. In each panel, from left to right, we respectively plot the results using FastICA-neg-entropy and JADE, followed by FastICA initialized respectively by naive matricization, random unit vectors, randomly sliced matricization, and Algorithm
2
.

Figure
1
shows the benefit of using randomization to initialize iteration algorithms for ICA. The standard algorithms of FastICA with negative entropy and JADE, when used with the deflation variant (as provided in
R
packages
FastICA
and
JADE
) do not show desirable performance even when the sample size is high. Similarly, the naive matricization method, which applies SVD to the
(1,2)
-matricization of the fourth-order cumulant tensor, does not perform well. As we point out in Section
2
, this is because there is no eigengap in this matrix, and consequently SVD is not unique. Algorithmically, the method ran into convergence issues for the same reason.

In contrast, the randomization-based methods perform increasingly well for moderately large sample sizes. We compared three such initialization schemes: a random unit vector, SVD of a random linear combination of the
(1,2)
slices of the fourth-order moment tensor (as done by
Anandkumar et al. 2014b
), as well as our method based on the improved fourth-order moment estimate (Algorithm
2
). Note that the improved moment estimates perform better than the other two initialization algorithms. This trend is more pronounced in the high dimensional regime, given in Figures
3
,
4
, and
5
.

) is one of the better-performing methods in Figure

In the next set of numerical experiments, we increase the sample size and dimensionality and provide further numerical evidence corroborating our theoretical developments.

. The only difference between the two approaches is in the initialization step and our method uses an improved moment estimate. To evaluate the effect of this on the quality of estimated unmixing directions, we vary

from

5.3
Real Data Example

Finally, we consider a financial application of the proposed ICA method. Linear factor models are widely used to describe cross-sectional asset price movements and assess portfolio risks. See, e.g.,
Connor et al. 2010
. PCA and asymptotic PCA are routinely used to extract latent factors. However, recent empirical evidence has suggested that they are inadequate in capturing higher-order risk and many have advocated the use of ICA. See, e.g.,
Lassance and Vrins 2021
;
Lassance et al. 2022
and references therein. To illustrate the practical merits of our approach, we apply it to a benchmark dataset constructed by Ken French. The data contain monthly returns of 30 industrial portfolios from July 1972 to September 2022. Each portfolio takes equal weights for all companies from a certain industrial sector such as
Autos
,
Rtail
, and
Txtls
. The estimated factor loadings for the first four sources and their (asymptotic) 95% confidence intervals are given in Figure
7
.

From Figure
7
, it is interesting to note that the independent component vectors (ICs) form a separation of the industry sectors. For example, the significant coefficients in the first IC correspond to the consumer sector:
Games
-which stands for sports and entertainment;
Rtail
- the retail sector,
Meals
- which includes restaurants, motels etc.,
Paper
- for the paper and stationery products,
BusEq
- which stands for business equipment like computers, phones, radio, communication devices; and finally
Hshld
and
Beers
, which denote household items and alcoholic beverages respectively. The second IC has significant coefficients on
Books
-meaning the publishing industry of books, newspapers, periodicals etc; followed by
Autos
-standing for automobiles;
Steel
-referring to heavy industries involving steel; and finally
Meals
. We suspect that this IC is driven by the heavy industry sector but is somehow correlated with the publishing industry. The third IC has an energy component: as led by the significant coefficients for
Oil
(petroleum, natural gas etc.),
Coal
,
Mines
as well as
Txtls
-consisting of mills. Finally, the fourth IC once again has significant coefficients on
Txtls
,
Oil
,
Meals
, and
Autos
but is now accompanied by
ElcEq
-denoting electrical equipment in general, along with
Cnstr
-which stands for heavy construction materials.

6
Concluding Remarks

In this paper, we study the effect of dimensionality on statistical properties and computational complexity of ICA. Our work contributes in three different directions. First, we derive the information-theoretic lower bound on the sample complexity required to perform ICA in high dimensions. To accompany this lower bound we investigate why commonly used ICA techniques are necessarily suboptimal and propose a truncated tensor decomposition method that is indeed optimal. The proposed minimax optimal method is however computationally intractable which prompts us we study the optimal sample complexity under computational constraints. More specifically, under the low-degree polynomial framework of
Hopkins 2018
;
Kunisky et al. 2022
, we show that the computational sample complexity threshold is at
n\gg d^{2}
, up to a logarithmic factor and thus demonstrate a gap in the information-theoretic and computational thresholds.

Secondly, in Section
3
, we show how popular approaches such as FastICA may be suboptimal and identify initialization as the culprit in deriving computationally feasible and statistically efficient estimators in high dimensions. We demonstrate the shortcomings of initialization schemes prevalent in the literature and develop new initialization schemes that result in a minimax rate optimal estimator for ICA whenever
n\gg d^{2}
, matching the computational lower bound up to a logarithmic factor. Finally, in Section
4
, we derive the asymptotic distributions of our estimators which can be readily used for statistical inferences.

We now point out a few related questions that should be investigated further. Firstly, note that for simplicity we have assumed that the observations are pre-whitened. In other words, we assume that the data are isotropic and the mixing matrices are orthonormal. In general, we need to estimate the covariance matrix first, before applying our proposed method. In Section 3.4, we also show that these two steps can be performed with sample splitting. It is possible that this data splitting is not essential, and we leave this question as a possible topic for future research.

More importantly, it is imperative to suggest methods of performing ICA for problems of even higher dimensions. We show that without further assumptions
n\asymp d^{2}
is the correct computational threshold. However, it is possible that this sample complexity result can be improved greatly if one assumes more structure on the mixing matrices. For example, sparsity is a popular assumption of this nature. It would be interesting to devise an ICA algorithm that depends optimally on the sparsity level while having minimal dependence on the dimension
d
. Along this direction, it would be interesting to consider a noisy version of the ICA problem, where instead of
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}
one observes
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}+\mathbf{E}
for some noise vector
\mathbf{E}
from some other distribution. This problem has been considered several times in the literature
(
Belkin et al. 2013
, see, e.g.,)
but an optimal dependence on the noise variance still remains elusive.

Finally, we point out the dependence of our methods on the moments of the independent sources. Following FastICA, we use an algorithm that measures departure from Gaussianity in terms of the fourth cumulants, i.e., the kurtosis. In principle, the same approach should provide a method of estimation whenever some higher-order cumulant is sufficiently different from zero (since all corresponding cumulants for the Gaussian distribution are zero). However, this is tied to the question of estimating an even higher-order moment tensor, which is presumed to be a computationally more difficult task. It would be interesting to determine this tradeoff between managing the computational burden vs making the moment assumptions less restrictive. For our analysis, we also assume finite
(8+\varepsilon)
-th moments for rate optimal estimation and finite 12th moments for asymptotic distributions. It might be possible to weaken these assumptions further by using some suitably truncated estimators, for example. We leave this question for future research.

Appendix A
Proofs

We now present the proofs of our main results. To facilitate the presentation, we defer the proofs of preliminary results and technical tools to Section
A.4
.

A.1
Proofs of Results in Section
2

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

For
1\leq k\leq d,
we take independent random variables

S_{k}=\alpha S_{k,1}+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}S_{k,2}

where
S_{k,1}\sim N(0,1)
and
S_{k,2}
is a Rademacher random variable. It is not hard to see that

|\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|=2(1-\alpha^{2})^{2}.

We consider the ICA model as
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S},
where
\mathbf{A}
is a
d\times d
orthogonal matrix, while
\mathbf{S}
is a
d
-dimensional vector consisting of i.i.d. random variables
S_{k}
as defined above.

By standard results on packing numbers of orthonormal matrices,
(
Szarek 1997
, see e.g.,)
, it is possible to construct a set
{\cal A}
of orthonormal matrices such that, for a sufficiently small
\delta>0
, we have

\sqrt{d\delta/2}\leq\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{(j)}\|_{\rm F}\leq\sqrt{d\delta}

(21)

for
i\neq j
and
\mathbf{A}^{(i)},\mathbf{A}^{(j)}\in{\cal A}
and

|{\cal A}|\geq 3^{d^{2}}

(22)

Note that the density function of each
S_{k}
is

\displaystyle f(s)=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{2\sqrt{2\pi}\alpha}\exp(-(s-\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}})^{2}/2\alpha^{2})+\dfrac{1}{2\sqrt{2\pi}\alpha}\exp(-(s+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}})^{2}/2\alpha^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\alpha}\exp\left(-\dfrac{s^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}}\right)\cdot\cosh\left(\dfrac{s\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\cdot\exp\left(-\dfrac{(1-\alpha^{2})}{2\alpha^{2}}\right).

It follows that for two mixing matrices
\mathbf{P}\neq\mathbf{Q}\in{\cal O}(d)
, we have the joint pdf’s for
\mathbf{X}
are given by

g_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{x})=\prod_{k=1}^{d}f(\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{x})\,\,\text{ and }g_{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{x})=\prod_{k=1}^{d}f(\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{x})

when
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{Q}
respectively.
We then have

\displaystyle\dfrac{g_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{x})}{g_{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{x})}=

\displaystyle~\prod_{k=1}^{d}\left(\exp\left(-\dfrac{(\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{x})^{2}-(\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{x})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}}\right)\cdot\dfrac{\cosh(\mathbf{x}^{\top}\mathbf{p}_{k}\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}/\alpha^{2})}{\cosh(\mathbf{x}^{\top}\mathbf{q}_{k}\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}/\alpha^{2})}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\prod_{k=1}^{d}\dfrac{\cosh(\mathbf{x}^{\top}\mathbf{p}_{k}\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}/\alpha^{2})}{\cosh(\mathbf{x}^{\top}\mathbf{q}_{k}\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}/\alpha^{2})}.

(23)

The second equality uses the fact that
\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}
are orthonormal matrices, and thus

\|\mathbf{P}\mathbf{x}\|^{2}=\|\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{x}\|^{2}
. When
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}
and we consider
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{A}_{i}
and
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{A}_{j}
for some
\mathbf{A}^{(i)},\mathbf{A}^{(j)}\in{\cal A}
and
i\neq j
, it follows that

\mathbf{a}^{(i)\top}_{k}\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{A}^{(j)}\mathbf{S}=\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(j)}S_{k}+\sum_{l\neq k}\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\mathbf{S}_{l}.

Consequently, the Kullback-Leibler divergence, when we observe
\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
, can be bounded as

\displaystyle~{\rm KL}(g_{\mathbf{A}^{(i)}};g_{\mathbf{A}^{(j)}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\mathbb{E}_{g_{\mathbf{A}^{(i)}}}\left(\log\left(\dfrac{g_{\mathbf{A}^{(i)}}(\mathbf{X})}{g_{\mathbf{A}^{(j)}}(\mathbf{X})}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left(\log(\cosh(S_{k}\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}/\alpha^{2}))-\log\left(\cosh\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}}{\alpha^{2}}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{d}\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle S_{l}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\dfrac{{\rm cum}_{2m}(R)}{(2m)!}\left(\mathbb{E}(S_{k}\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}/\alpha^{2})^{2m}-\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}}{\alpha^{2}}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{d}\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle S_{l}\right)^{2m}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\dfrac{{\rm cum}_{2m}(R)(1-\alpha^{2})^{m}}{(2m)!\alpha^{4m}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(S_{k}^{2m}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{d}\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle S_{l}\right)^{2m}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\dfrac{{\rm cum}_{2m}(R)(1-\alpha^{2})^{m}}{(2m)!\alpha^{4m}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\alpha Z+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}R_{k}\right)^{2m}-\mathbb{E}\left(\alpha Z+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle R_{l}\right)^{2m}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\dfrac{{\rm cum}_{2m}(R)(1-\alpha^{2})^{m}}{(2m)!\alpha^{4m}}\left(\mathbb{E}X_{k}^{2m}-\mathbb{E}Y_{k}^{2m}\right)

(24)

where we define the random variables within the summation as
X_{k}
and
Y_{k}
respectively. The third equality uses the fact that
\log(\cosh(x))
is the cumulant generating function of Rademacher random variables. We also use the notation
Z
and
R_{k}
to mean independent
N(0,1)
and Rademacher random variables respectively. Note that

X_{k}-Y_{k}=\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}\left((\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(j)}\rangle-1)R_{k}+\sum_{l\neq k}\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle R_{l}\right).

Note that
\mathbb{E}(X_{k}-Y_{k})^{2m+1}=0
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
and any
k\in[d]
, and

\mathbb{E}(X_{k}-Y_{k})^{2}={\rm Var}(X_{k}-Y_{k})=(1-\alpha^{2})\|\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(j)}\|^{2}
.

Note that
Z,R_{1},\dots,R_{k}
are independent random variables which are symmetric around zero. Thus
\mathbb{E}(\alpha Z+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}R_{k})^{2m-1}R_{j}=0
for all
j\neq k
, and moreover

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\alpha Z+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}R_{k})^{2m-1}R_{k}=

\displaystyle~\sum_{l=0}^{2m-1}{{2m-1}\choose{l}}(\alpha)^{2m-1-l}(1-\alpha^{2})^{l/2}\mathbb{E}Z^{2m-1-l}\mathbb{E}R_{k}^{l+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{s=1}^{m}{{2m-1}\choose{2s-1}}(\alpha)^{2m-2s}(1-\alpha^{2})^{(2s-1)/2}\mathbb{E}Z^{2m-2s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}(\mathbb{E}(1+\alpha Z)^{2m-1}-1).

Then by binomial series expansion of
(Y_{k}-X_{k}+X_{k})^{2m}
, it can be checked that for any
m\geq 3
,

\displaystyle~|\mathbb{E}X_{k}^{2m}-\mathbb{E}Y_{k}^{2m}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}\cdot 2m\cdot(1-\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(j)}\rangle)\mathbb{E}\left((\alpha Z+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}R_{k})^{2m-1}R_{k}\right)

\displaystyle~+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{l=2}^{2m}{{2m}\choose{l}}(1-\alpha^{2})^{l/2}\|\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(j)}\|^{l}(\alpha Z+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}R_{k})^{2m-l}\left(R_{k}+\dfrac{1}{\|\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\|}\sum_{l\neq k}\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle R_{l}\right)^{l}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~(1-\alpha^{2})\|\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(j)}\|^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left(1+\alpha Z+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}\left(2R_{k}+\dfrac{1}{\|\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\|}\sum_{l\neq k}\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle R_{l}\right)\right)^{2m}.

Plugging this back into (
A.1
), we have

\displaystyle~{\rm KL}(g_{\mathbf{A}^{(i)}};g_{\mathbf{A}^{(j)}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\dfrac{{\rm cum}_{2m}(R)(1-\alpha^{2})^{m}}{(2m)!\alpha^{4m}}\left(\mathbb{E}X_{k}^{2m}-\mathbb{E}Y_{k}^{2m}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\dfrac{-2(1-\alpha^{2})^{2}}{4!\alpha^{8}}\cdot(1-\alpha^{2})^{2}\left(1-\sum_{l=1}^{d}\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt~+n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{m=3}^{\infty}\dfrac{{\rm cum}_{2m}(R)(1-\alpha^{2})^{m}}{(2m)!\alpha^{4m}}\left(\mathbb{E}X_{k}^{2m}-\mathbb{E}Y_{k}^{2m}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\dfrac{4(1-\alpha^{2})^{2}}{4!\alpha^{8}}\cdot(1-\alpha^{2})^{2}(1-\langle\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k},\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\rangle^{2})+n\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{m=3}^{\infty}\dfrac{{\rm cum}_{2m}(R)(1-\alpha^{2})^{m+1}}{(2m)!\alpha^{4m}}\|\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(j)}\|^{2}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\mathbb{E}\left(1+\alpha Z+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}\left(2R_{k}+\sum_{l\neq k}\dfrac{\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\|}R_{l}\right)\right)^{2m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~4n(1-\alpha^{2})^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}\log\cosh\left(1/\alpha^{2}Z/\alpha+\dfrac{\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}}{\alpha^{2}}\left(2R_{k}+\sum_{l\neq k}\dfrac{\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\|}R_{l}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~4n(1-\alpha^{2})^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\lvert 1/\alpha^{2}+Z/\alpha+\dfrac{\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}}{\alpha^{2}}\left(2R_{k}+\sum_{l\neq k}\dfrac{\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{a}_{l}^{(j)}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\|}R_{l}\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~4n(1-\alpha^{2})^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|\mathbf{a}^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbf{a}^{(j)}_{k}\|^{2}\cdot\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{2}}+\dfrac{3(1-\alpha^{2})+1}{\alpha^{4}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~8n(1-\alpha^{2})^{4}\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{(j)}\|_{\rm F}^{2}/\alpha\leq 8n(1-\alpha^{2})^{4}(d\delta)/\alpha.

The first inequality follows by the upper bound we derived earlier. The next step uses the power series expansion of
\log(\cosh(x))
. The third inequality uses the fact that
\cosh(x)\leq\exp(|x|)
. The fourth inequality is an application of Cauchy-Schwarz, while the last two steps involve the assumption
\alpha^{2}>2/3
.

By the generalized Fano inequality
(
Yu 1997
, see Lemma 3 of )
, we then have

\underset{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}{\inf}\underset{\mathbf{A}\in{\cal A}}{\sup}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{\rm F}\geq\sqrt{\dfrac{d\delta}{2}}\left(1-\dfrac{8n(1-\alpha^{2})^{4}d\delta/\alpha+\log 2}{d^{2}\log 3}\right).

We now take

\delta=\dfrac{d\alpha}{10(1-\alpha^{2})^{4}n}

to get that

\underset{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}{\inf}\underset{\mathbf{A}\in{\cal A}}{\sup}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{\rm F}\geq c\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}\alpha^{4}}{(1-\alpha^{2})^{4}n}}\geq\dfrac{c}{\min_{1\leq k\leq d}|\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|}\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}.

(25)

This finishes the proof for the
\ell_{A}
error. The lower bound on the maximum columnwise error follows immediately by noting that

\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\mathbf{a}_{j}\|\geq\left(\dfrac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\mathbf{a}_{j}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\cdot\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\rm F}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.2
.

From the independent samples
\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
, let us define

\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{X}_{1}/\|\mathbf{X}_{1}\|.

Note that
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Hence

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}-\mathscr{M}_{4}\|=

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{4}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{4}\right\rvert\geq\left\lvert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{n}\cdot\langle\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4}-\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{4}=\dfrac{\|\mathbf{X}_{1}\|^{4}}{n}-\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{\|\mathbf{S}_{1}\|^{4}}{n}-\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{4}.

(26)

In the first inequality, we use a particular unit vector
\mathbf{v}
as defined earlier. The third inequality uses the fact that
\langle\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4}\geq 0
. The last two equalities follow since
\mathbf{X}_{1}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}_{1}
and
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}
, where
\mathbf{A}\in{\cal O}(d)
is an orthonormal matrix.

First, by Lemma
18
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{S}_{1}\|^{4}\geq d^{2}/4)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\|\mathbf{S}_{1}\|^{2}-d\right\rvert\leq d/2\right)\geq 1-Cd^{-(4+\epsilon/2)}.

On the other hand, for any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have from our assumptions that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{4}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d}u_{j}^{4}\mathbb{E}S_{j}^{4}+\sum_{j\neq k}u_{j}^{2}u_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}(S_{j}^{2})\mathbb{E}(S_{k}^{2})\leq\max\mathbb{E}S_{j}^{4}+1.

Plugging in these bounds into (
A.1
) implies that

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}-\mathscr{M}_{4}\|\geq\dfrac{d^{2}}{4n}-(\max\mathbb{E}S_{j}^{4}+1)\right)\geq 1-Cd^{-(4+\epsilon/2)}.

(27)

Let us define

\mathscr{X}:=\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0};\,\,\mathscr{T}:=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\circ 4};\,\,\mathscr{E}:=\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}).

Note that

\mathscr{X}=\mathscr{T}+\mathscr{E}
. By equation (
27
) we have that

\|\mathscr{E}\|=\|\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})\|\geq\dfrac{d^{2}}{8n}\geq 4

(28)

with probability at least
1-d^{-4-\epsilon/2}
, whenever
n\leq d^{2}/32
. By the definition of
\widehat{\kappa}_{4}(\mathbf{X})
, this proves the first part of the theorem. Let

\mathbf{u}_{*}=\underset{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}{\rm argmax}\,|\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{v}|.

By definition of
\mathbf{u}_{*}
, it then follows that

\displaystyle\lambda=|\mathscr{X}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{u}_{*}|=

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|\mathscr{X}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{v}|\geq|\mathscr{X}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{v}_{*}|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~|\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{v}_{*}|-|\mathscr{T}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{v}_{*}|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\|\mathscr{E}\|-\left\lvert\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\langle\mathbf{a}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{*}\rangle^{4}\right\rvert.

(29)

Let
j=\underset{1\leq k\leq d}{\rm argmax}|\langle\mathbf{a}_{k},\mathbf{u}_{*}\rangle|
. We write

\mathbf{u}_{*}=\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\mathbf{a}_{j}+\rho\mathbf{v}

where
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{a}_{j}
, while
0<\rho<1
. We consider only the case where
\langle\mathbf{a}_{j},\mathbf{u}_{*}\rangle>0
, since the sign of
\mathbf{u}_{*}
is not determined here. Let us define the event

{\cal A}\equiv\left(\Delta_{1}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{C_{0}d(\log d)}{n}},\,\,\Delta_{2}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{C_{0}d(\log d)}{n}},\,\,\Delta_{3}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{C_{0}d(\log d)}{n}}+\dfrac{Cd^{13/8}(\log d)}{n^{7/8}}\right)

(30)

where
C_{0}
is a numerical constant, while
\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{X}-\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\circ 4}
, and

\Delta_{q}=\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert\mathscr{E}\times_{[4]/[q]}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right\rVert\text{ for }q=1,2,3.

By Lemmas
13
,
14
and
15
, we have that

\mathbb{P}({\cal A})\geq 1-d^{-3}.

Then under the event
{\cal A}
, we have the upper bound as follows:

\displaystyle\lambda=

\displaystyle~~|\mathscr{X}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{u}_{*}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~~|\mathscr{T}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{u}_{*}|+~|\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{u}_{*}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lvert\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\langle\mathbf{a}_{k},\mathbf{u}_{*}\rangle^{4}\right\rvert+(1-\rho^{2})^{2}|\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}|+4(1-\rho^{2})^{3/2}\rho\|\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2,3}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|

\displaystyle+6(1-\rho^{2})\rho^{2}\|\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|+4\rho^{3}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\|\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|+\rho^{4}\|\mathscr{E}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\kappa_{4}(S_{j})(1-\rho^{2})^{2}+\max_{k}{\kappa_{4}}(S_{k})\rho^{3}+4\rho\Delta_{1}+6\rho^{2}\Delta_{2}+4\rho^{3}\Delta_{3}+\rho^{4}\|\mathscr{E}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\kappa_{4}(S_{j})(1-\rho^{2})^{2}+\max_{k}{\kappa_{4}}(S_{k})\rho^{3}+C\rho\sqrt{\dfrac{d\log d}{n}}+\dfrac{C\rho^{3}d^{13/8}(\log d)}{n^{7/8}}+\rho^{4}\|\mathscr{E}\|.

Comparing this to the lower bound from (
A.1
) implies that

\displaystyle(1-\rho^{4})\|\mathscr{E}\|\leq(1+(1-\rho^{2})^{2}+\rho^{3})\max_{k}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})+C\sqrt{\dfrac{d(\log d)}{n}}+\dfrac{C\rho^{3}d^{13/8}(\log d)}{n^{7/8}}

and hence

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle~\left\{\dfrac{1}{\|\mathscr{E}\|}\cdot\left(\max_{k}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})+C\sqrt{\dfrac{d(\log d)}{n}}+\dfrac{C\rho^{3}d^{13/8}(\log d)}{n^{7/8}}\right)\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\{\dfrac{8n}{d^{2}}\cdot\left(\max_{k}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})+C\sqrt{\dfrac{d(\log d)}{n}}+\dfrac{Cd^{13/8}(\log d)}{n^{7/8}}\right)\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\dfrac{C\sqrt{\log d}}{d^{1/8}},

whenever
n\leq d^{2}/C
for a constant
C>0
. In the second inequality above, we use the lower bound on
\|\mathscr{E}\|
from (
28
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.3
.

We will first prove an upper bound on the error norm
\|\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})\|
. It follows from the moment assumption and Lemma
19
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\E\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{X}\rangle^{8}=\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{S}\rangle^{8}\leq L

for some constant
L>0
. Thus

{\rm Var}(\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4})\leq L^{\prime}

for some constant
L^{\prime}>0
. Then we apply Proposition 2.4 of
Catoni 2012
to obtain that, for any
t\geq\exp(-n/2)
, we have

\left|\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbf{u}}-\E(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i})^{4}\right|\leq\sqrt{2L\log(t^{-1})\over n\left(1-2\log(t^{-1})/n\right)},

(31)

with probability at least
1-2t
.

It is clear that
\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})
is in the feasible set of the optimization. We now apply (
31
) with
t=\exp(-3d)
to each
\mathbf{u}\in{\cal N}
. Taking the union bound over all
\mathbf{u}\in{\cal N}
, we have with probability at least

1-|{\cal N}|\exp(-3d)=1-\exp(-(3-\log 9)d)\geq 1-2.2^{-d},

that

\underset{\mathbf{u}\in{\cal N}}{\max}|\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbf{u}}-\langle{\cal M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}),\,\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}|\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{6Ld}{n(1-6d/n)}}.

Under this event, for any
\mathbf{u}\in{\cal N},

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{{\cal M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-{\cal M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}),\,\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbf{u}}-\langle\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X}),\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|+\left|\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbf{u}}-\langle\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}),\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{\mathbf{u}\in{\cal N}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbf{u}}-\langle\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}),\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 5\sqrt{Ld\over n\left(1-6d/n\right)}.

from which we can conclude that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{u}\in{\cal S}^{d-1}}{\sup}\left|\langle\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}),\,\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{u}\in{\cal N}}{\sup}\left|\langle\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}),\,\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|+\dfrac{1}{4}\underset{\mathbf{u}\in{\cal S}^{d-1}}{\sup}\left|\langle\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}),\,\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|

which means that

\|\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})\|\leq 7\sqrt{Ld\over n\left(1-6d/n\right)}

with probability at least
1-1.4^{-d}.
We now apply the tensor perturbation bounds from Theorems 2.3 and 2.4 of
Auddy and Yuan 2023
to obtain

\ell_{M}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{A})\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\dfrac{d}{n}}.

Note that
\ell_{A}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{A})\leq\ell_{M}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{A})
and this finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.4
.

Let

\mathbf{Z}=(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{Z}_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d},

be a matrix of i.i.d. standard Gaussian elements
Z_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}N(0,1)
. We consider the polynomial basis

h_{m}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}/\|\mathbf{X}\|)\quad\text{ for }m\in[D],\,\,j\in[d],\,\,\text{and}\,\,i\in[n].

Here
h_{m}
is the
m^{\text{th}}
probabilist’s Hermite polynomial. We define

\psi_{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{d}}^{(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\left({h_{\alpha_{i,j}}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}/\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\|)}-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}{h_{\alpha_{i,j}}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}/\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\|)}\right)

for
0\leq\alpha_{i,j}\leq D
. Note that this is a random polynomial basis. Since
\mathbf{Z}_{j}
are independent, it can be checked that this basis is incoherent. That is, there exists
0<\delta<1
such that for any polynomial
f
in
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}
, that has degree at most
C\log d
, we have

(1-\delta)\langle f,f\rangle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{N}\langle f,\psi_{t}\rangle^{2}\leq(1+\delta)\langle f,f\rangle.

We will now show that Lemma
1
implies the non-existence of any consistent estimator of
\mathbf{A}
that is constructed from low-degree polynomials of
\mathbf{X}_{i}
. To see this, suppose we have an estimator

\widehat{\mathbf{a}}={\rm poly}_{\leq(D-1)/4}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\text{ for }i=1,\dots,n-1\right).

For the sake of contradiction, suppose
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}
is a consistent estimator of
\mathbf{a}_{j}
. Then, for
\mathbf{P}\in{\cal O}(d)
, consider the low-degree polynomial

f_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})=(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{n})^{4}-(\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|\mathbf{p}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{n})^{4}.

Lemma 6
.

For any
\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\in{\cal O}(d)
, there exist constants
0<\eta_{1},\eta_{2}<1
depending only on
\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{6}/\kappa_{4}(S_{1})
, such that

\dfrac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{Q}}f_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{P}}f_{j}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})}}\geq 4

if
\ell(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q})=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{A}}\left(\|\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}-\mathbf{a}_{j}\|\leq\eta_{1}\right)\geq 1-\eta_{2}
for some
1\leq j\leq d
under
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{Q}
.

Since
f_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\in\mathbb{R}_{\leq D}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n})
, equation (
9
) and Lemma
6
give a contradiction to Lemma
1
whenever
n\leq d^{2}/(CD^{2}(\log d)^{5})
. We thus arrive at the given computational minimax lower bound.
∎

A.2
Proofs of Results in Section
3

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

We define the target matrix

\mathbf{M}=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}.

Note that

\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{G}_{s})={\rm unvec}(\mathbf{M}{\rm vec}(\mathbf{G}_{s}))=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})Z_{k}(\mathbf{G}_{s})\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}.

where for each fixed
s
, we have
Z_{k}(\mathbf{G}_{s})=\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{s}\mathbf{a}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}N(0,1)
, since
\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k}
are orthogonal unit vectors, and
{\rm vec}(\mathbf{G}_{s})\sim N(\mathbf{0},\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}})
. Let us define

\widehat{\mathbf{P}}={\rm Proj}_{d}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}-{\cal M}_{(12)(3,4)}\left(\mathscr{M}_{0}\right)\right)

the rank-
d
projection of the matrix
\widehat{\mathbf{M}}-{\cal M}_{(12)(3,4)}\left(\mathscr{M}_{0}\right)
. Our estimator can be written as

\widehat{\mathscr{M}}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}G=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}{\rm unvec}(\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{G})\mathbf{a}_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}+\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{G}.

We further expand the rightmost hand side to write

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{M}}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}G=

\displaystyle~\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}{\rm unvec}(\mathbf{G})\mathbf{a}_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}{\rm unvec}((\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{G})\mathbf{a}_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}+\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{G},

(32)

where
\mathbf{P}=\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{d}(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}
. We will first bound the second term in the above expression. Note that

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}{\rm unvec}((\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{G})\mathbf{a}_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}{\rm unvec}((\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{G})\mathbf{a}_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{G}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\left\lVert(\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})\right\rVert\sqrt{\log d}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log d)}{n}}

with probability at least
1-d^{-3}
. Here we use Gaussian concentration inequalities and a union bound in the second last step, since
\mathbf{G}\sim N(0,\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}})
. We also apply Lemma
3
in the last step.

Moving on to the third term in (
A.2
) we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{G}\right\rVert=

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\left(\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\right)\times_{3,4}\mathbf{G}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\sum_{i,j}g_{ij}\left(\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\right)_{..ij}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\max\left\{\left\lVert{\cal M}_{(1)(234)}(\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}})\right\rVert,\left\lVert{\cal M}_{(2)(134)}(\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}})\right\rVert\right\}\times\sqrt{\log d}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}\log d}{n}}.

In the second step, we write
..ij
to refer to the
(i,j)
th
(3,4)
slice of
\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}
. The next step uses concentration inequalities for matrix Gaussian sequences from
Tropp et al. 2015
. In the last step we apply Lemma
4
. Plugging these inequalities back into (
A.2
), we then have

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathscr{M}}\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{G}-\sum_{k=1}^{d}((\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G})\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq C\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log d)}{n}}\right)

(33)

with high probability. Next, since
\mathbf{a}_{k}
are orthogonal, it follows that
Z_{k}:=(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}N(0,1)
. Suppose now that we repeatedly sample
\mathbf{G}_{s}
for
s=1,\dots,L
. By anti-concentration bounds for Gaussian random variables,
(
Anandkumar et al. 2014c
, see Lemma B.1 of )
, (fixing
k=1
) without loss of generality, we have that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq s\leq L}(\mathbf{a}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{1})^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{s}\leq\left(\sqrt{2\log L}-\dfrac{\log\log L}{4\sqrt{\log L}}-\sqrt{\log 8}\right)\right)\leq\dfrac{1}{4}.

(34)

Note that by orthogonality of
\mathbf{a}_{k}
, we have

(\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{s}\text{ and }(\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}}-(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top})(\mathbf{G}_{s})\text{ are independent}.

Let

s_{*}=\argmax\limits_{1\leq s\leq L}\kappa_{4}(S_{1})(\mathbf{a}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{1})^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{s}.

Since the definition of
s_{*}
depends only on
(\mathbf{a}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{1})^{\top}\mathbf{G}
, this implies that the distribution of
(\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}}-(\mathbf{a}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{1})(\mathbf{a}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{1})^{\top})(\mathbf{G})
does not depend on
s_{*}
. Thus we obtain the eigengap

\displaystyle~\kappa_{4}(S_{1})(\mathbf{a}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{1})^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{s_{*}}-\max_{2\leq k\leq d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{s_{*}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\left(\sqrt{2\log L}-\dfrac{\log\log L}{4\sqrt{\log L}}-\sqrt{\log 8}-2\sqrt{\log(d/4)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\log d}

with probability at least
\frac{1}{2}
, whenever
L\geq Cd^{2}
and
n\geq Cd^{2}t^{2}
. Since
\mathbf{G}_{s}
are independent samples, we instead take
L=Cd^{2}(\log d)
. Then writing
L=L_{1}+\dots+L_{t}
, for
t=3(\log d)/(\log 2)
, it follows that
L_{1},\dots,L_{k}\geq Cd
. We define
s_{*}={\rm argmax}~\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{a}_{k}
, and
s_{*}^{(m)}={\rm argmax}_{1\leq s\leq L_{m}}~\langle\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k},\bm{\theta}_{s}\rangle
for
m=1,\dots,t
. We then have, by the independence of
\bm{\theta}_{s}
, that the above statement holds with probability
1-d^{-3}
. Referring back to (
33
), we then apply Davis-Kahan theorem to obtain that

\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a}_{1}\right)\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}\leq\dfrac{1}{4}

with probability at least
1-d^{-3}
, provided
n\geq Cd^{2}
. Here
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}
is the top singular vector of
(\widehat{\mathscr{M}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{G}_{s_{*}}
. This finishes the proof of the initialization.
∎

When accounting for the deflation step, we have an additional perturbation term in the gaussian matrix above. This can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle~\left\lVert(\sum_{k=1}^{j}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\circ 4}-\sum_{k=1}^{j}\widehat{\kappa}_{4}(S_{k})\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}^{\circ 4})\times_{3,4}\mathbf{G}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{j}\left\lvert\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}-\widehat{\kappa}_{4}(S_{k})(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}\right\rvert\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{j}\widehat{\kappa}_{4}(S_{k})(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}\left\{\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}^{\top}-\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\right\}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\max_{1\leq k\leq j}\left\lvert\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}-\widehat{\kappa}_{4}(S_{k})(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}\right\rvert+\max_{1\leq k\leq j}\widehat{\kappa}_{4}(S_{k})(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k})^{\top}\mathbf{G}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{j}\left\{\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}^{\top}-\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\right\}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\max_{1\leq k\leq j}\left\lvert\kappa_{4}(S_{k})-\widehat{\kappa}_{4}(S_{k})\right\rvert\sqrt{C\log d}+\max_{1\leq k\leq j}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\max_{1\leq k\leq j}\left(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{G}

\displaystyle~+\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{j}\left\{\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{k}^{\top}-\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\top}\right\}\right\rVert\times\sqrt{C\log d}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sqrt{\dfrac{(d+d^{2}\mathbbm{1}(\epsilon<4))t^{2}}{n}}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-t)-n^{-\epsilon/8}
. The last line follows using Theorem
3.3
and Lemma
10
on the previously recovered terms
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}
. Since
n\geq Cd^{2}
, this finishes the proof.

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

Let us define the noise tensor

\mathscr{E}:=\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X}).

(35)

Define

\Delta:=\|\mathscr{E}\|;\Delta_{1}:=\max\limits_{j}\|\mathscr{E}\times_{q\neq 1,2,3}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|;\,\,\Delta_{2}:=\max\limits_{j}\|\mathscr{E}\times_{q\neq 1,2}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|;\text{ and }\,\Delta_{3}:=\|\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|.

(36)

By Lemmas
2
,
14
and
15
, we have that for any fixed
j\in[d]
, and any
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\Delta_{3}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd(\log(1/\delta))}{n}},\,\,\Delta_{2}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd(\log d)}{n}},\,\,\Delta\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{C(d\log\delta)^{2}}{n}}\right)\geq 1-\delta-d^{-3}-n^{-\epsilon/8}.

(37)

By the initialization procedure, and also since we choose the largest singular value at the end of each iteration, we have nontrivial initialization for the component satisfying

\kappa_{4}(S_{j})=\max_{i\geq j}\kappa_{4}(S_{i})
. We will use the notation

\kappa_{i}:=|\kappa_{4}(S_{i})|\,\,\text{ for }i=1,\dots,d.

Let

\eta_{t}:=\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]},\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)
.
By the calculation preceding the statement of Theorem
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\eta_{t}\leq

\displaystyle~2|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|^{-1}\Delta_{3}+6|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|^{-1}\Delta_{2}\eta_{t-1}+6|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|^{-1}\Delta_{1}\eta_{t-1}^{2}+2|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|^{-1}\Delta\eta_{t-1}^{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{2}{|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|}\Delta_{3}+\eta_{t-1}\left(\dfrac{6}{|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|}\sqrt{\dfrac{C(d\log d)}{n}}+\dfrac{6\eta_{t-1}}{|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|}\sqrt{\dfrac{C(d\log\delta)^{2}}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{2\Delta_{3}}{|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|}+\dfrac{\eta_{t-1}}{2},

by (
36
) and (
37
) in the last two steps along with the induction hypothesis, since
n\geq Cd^{2}(\log\delta)^{2}
, along with our initialization assumption
L_{0}\leq 1/4
. Thus repeating the argument above, it can be shown that
\eta_{t-1}\leq 1/4
, and after
T\geq C(\log d)
steps, we have, under the event in (
37
) that

\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]},\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)=\eta_{T}\leq\dfrac{3\Delta_{3}}{|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|}.

(38)

The proof now follows by the upper bound on
\Delta_{3}
from Lemma
5
. ∎

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

We prove the statements by induction over
j
, the iteration counter of
Algorithm
1
. By the induction hypothesis and (
38
), we already have estimators such that

\max_{1\leq k\leq j-1}\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(k)},\mathbf{a}_{k}\right)\leq\dfrac{3\Delta_{3}}{\kappa_{4}(S_{j-1})}

with high probability. Applying Theorem
3.1
, we obtain that at the
j^{\text{th}}
step, we have

\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}^{(s)},\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)\leq\min\left\{\dfrac{1}{C},\dfrac{\min_{k}|\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|}{2\max_{k}|\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|}\right\}

for at least one
s\in[L]
, and some
\mathbf{a}_{j}
which has not been estimated previously. Now we apply the power iteration steps with
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[0]}:=\widehat{\mathbf{a}}^{(s)}
. Theorem
3.2
then implies that
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}:=\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]}
satisfies

\mathbb{P}\left(\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

It follows that this estimator
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}
satisfies

\displaystyle~|(\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{1,2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~|({\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{1,2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}|-\|\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~|\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a}_{k}\rangle^{4}|-C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log 1/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\kappa_{4}(S_{j})(1-\sin^{2}\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a}_{j}\right))^{4}-|\max\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)-C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log 1/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|-C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log 1/\delta)}{n}}.

We have used Lemma
2
in the second inequality. Note that we consider the best initialization
s\in[L]
by using
s_{*}:={\rm argmax}|(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{1,2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]}^{(s)}|
. By the calculation above, it is immediate that

|(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{1,2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]}^{(s_{*})}|\geq|\kappa_{4}(S_{j})|-C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log 1/\delta)}{n}}.

(39)

If
|\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]}^{s_{*}},\mathbf{a}_{k}\rangle|\leq\dfrac{\min|\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|}{4\max|\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|}
for all
k\in[d]
, we have that

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{1,2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]}^{(s_{*})}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\|\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})\|+|({\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{1,2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]}^{(s_{*})}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log 1/\delta)}{n}}+\sum_{k}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]}^{(s)},\mathbf{a}_{k}\rangle)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log 1/\delta)}{n}}+\max\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\cdot\max_{k\in[d]}(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[T]}^{(s)},\mathbf{a}_{k}\rangle)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log 1/\delta)}{n}}+\dfrac{\min|\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|}{4}\leq\min|\kappa_{4}(S_{k})|/2,

which contradicts (
39
) above. This implies that for
s=s_{*}
, the initialization condition from Theorem
3.1
must be satisfied. We next apply the power iteration argument from Theorem
3.2
to claim that

\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)},\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)\leq\dfrac{3\Delta_{3}}{\kappa_{4}(S_{j})}.

This finishes the proof for
\ell_{M}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}},\mathbf{A})
by an induction argument along with the upper bound on
\Delta_{3}
from Lemma
5
. Finally noting that
\ell_{A}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}},\mathbf{A})\leq\ell_{M}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}},\mathbf{A})
the proof is completed.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.4
.

Let us write the SVD
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
. Then
\bm{\Sigma}=\texttt{cov}(\mathbf{X})=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{D}^{2}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}=\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
is an orthogonal mixing matrix. Thus
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}=\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}
are observations from an ICA model with orthonormal mixing matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
, on which our results from the previous section will apply. To simplify notation we write
n_{1}=|{\cal S}_{1}|
and
n_{2}=|{\cal S}_{2}|
.

We have defined

\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}=\dfrac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in S_{1}}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}.

It follows by a truncated matrix Bernstein inequality that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lVert\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\right\rVert\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{dt}{n_{1}}}\right)\leq\exp(-t)+n_{1}^{-\gamma/8}.

(40)

We now show that it is possible to derive versions of the concentration inequalities in Lemmas
2
-
5
as follows. For brevity, we outline the proof of only Lemma
2
as the rest follow similarly. We define

\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{i}=\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}_{i},\text{ for }i\in S_{2}.

Note that
\mathbf{X}_{i}
are independent of
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}
, and hence, conditional on
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}
,

\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{i}=\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}_{i}=\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{S}_{i}

are independent samples for
i\in S_{2}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in S_{2}}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\circ 4}-\left(\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}^{\circ 4}+\mathscr{M}_{0}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}\right\rVert\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\circ 4}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{S}^{\circ 4}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\left((\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}-\mathbb{I}_{d})\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}^{\circ 4}|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n_{2}}}+C\sqrt{\dfrac{dt_{3}}{n_{1}}}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-t_{1}-t_{3})-\dfrac{1}{Cn_{1}^{\gamma/8}(t_{2}\wedge t_{3})^{2+\gamma/4}}
using Lemma
2
for the first bound and equation (
40
) for the second bound. In an identical fashion, we can also prove the upper bounds on the required matricizations of
\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{i})
conditional on
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}
. Proceeding this way the conclusions from Lemmas
14
and
15
follow. Therefore, if we define

\mathscr{E}=\dfrac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in S_{2}}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\circ 4}-\mathscr{M}_{0}-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}_{k}^{\circ 4},

we have that for

\Delta_{q}:=\max\limits_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathscr{E}\times_{[4]/[4-q]}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|

for
q=1,2,3
, the event

{\cal A}\equiv\left(\|\mathscr{E}\|\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd^{2}(\log\delta)^{2}}{n_{2}}},\,\,\Delta_{1}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd^{2}(\log\delta)^{2}}{n_{2}}},\,\,\Delta_{2}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd(\log d)}{n_{2}}},\,\,\Delta_{3}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd(\log(1/\delta))}{n_{2}}}\right)

(41)

holds with probability at least
1-\delta-d^{-3}-n_{2}^{-\gamma/8}
. One can then follow the proof of Theorem
3.3
to get the conclusion:

\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)},\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)}\right)\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d(\log(1/\delta))}{n_{2}}}

(42)

with probability at least
1-\delta-d^{-3}-n_{2}^{-\gamma/8}
, for some permutation
\pi:[d]\to[d]
, where
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}
are the outputs of Algorithm
1
when used on
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
defined as above. Finally note that we redefine

\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}:=\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}
. It follows that for the permutation
\pi
defined above,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert{\rm sign}(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle)\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)}-\mathbf{a}_{j}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert{\rm sign}(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle)\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)}-\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}_{j}\right\rVert+\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}-\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}_{j}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\|\cdot\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert{\rm sign}(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)},\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}_{j}\rangle)\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)}-\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)}\right\rVert+C\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sqrt{d(\log(1/\delta))\cdot\left(\dfrac{1}{n_{1}}+\dfrac{1}{n_{2}}\right)}

using the bounds from (
40
) and (
42
) above. This finishes the proof since
n_{1}=n_{2}=n/2
.
∎

A.3
Proofs of Results in Section
4

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

We assume without loss of generality that
\pi=Id
and that
\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j},\,\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle>0
for
1\leq j\leq d
. We define

\mathscr{E}=\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})

and then use the asymptotic expansion of
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}
from Lemma
17
.

By (
36
), Lemmas
2
,
14
and
15
, we have that the event

{\cal A}\equiv\left(\Delta_{0}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd}{n(\log d)}},\,\,\Delta_{3}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd(\log d)}{n}},\,\,\Delta_{2}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd(\log d)}{n}},\,\,\|\mathscr{E}\|\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd^{2}(\log d)^{2}}{n}}\right)

(43)

holds with probability at least
1-d^{-3}-n^{-\epsilon/8}
. Next note that we have

\mathbf{E}_{j}:=\mathbf{P}_{j,\perp}^{\top}\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}=\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{jk}^{3}(S_{1k}^{3}\dots S_{(j-1)k}^{3}\,0\,S_{(j+1)k}^{3}\,\dots S_{dk}^{3})^{\top}.

Then by a truncated version of the vector Bernstein inequality
(
Minsker 2017
, see)
, we have

\|\mathbf{E}_{j}\|\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d\log(d)}{n}}

with probability at least
1-d^{-3}-n^{-\epsilon/8}
.
Given a unit vector
\mathbf{u}
, we have

\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\mathbf{a}_{j})=\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}+\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle-1)

(44)

First, we use part i) of Lemma
17
to write that

|\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle-1)|\leq\dfrac{C\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle d\log(d)}{n}.

Under the event
{\cal A}
defined in (
43
), it follows from part ii) of Lemma
17
, that

\sup_{\mathbf{u}\perp\mathbf{a}_{j},\|\mathbf{u}\|=1}\left\lvert\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{Cd(\log d)^{2}}{n}

for all
\mathbf{u}
. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we thus have, for any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that

\left\lvert\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{Cd(\log d)^{2}\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\|}{n}.

Combining this with the bound along
\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle
, we have from (
44
) that

\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\mathbf{a}_{j})-\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{Cd(\log d)^{2}\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\|}{n}+\dfrac{C\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle d(\log d)}{n}.

(45)

It remains to use the randomness of
\mathscr{E}
and derive the asymptotic distribution. Note that

\mathscr{E}=\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{X}_{k}^{\circ 4}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\circ 4}.

Moreover
\mathbf{X}_{k}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,n
. Thus

\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}=~\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{A}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{kj}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{k}-\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1}\right).

(46)

Since
\mathbf{S}_{k}
have independent components
S_{kj}
for
k=1,\dots,n
and
j=1,\dots,d
, we obtain

{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1})={\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3})\cdot\mathbb{I}_{d}+({\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{4})-{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3}))\mathbf{e}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top},

Therefore

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\left(\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{A}S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1}\right)=\dfrac{{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3})}{\kappa_{j}^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}.

By our assumptions,

\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}S_{1j}^{3}\right\rvert^{4}\right)\leq C
. Similarly it follows by Lemma
19
that

\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\langle\mathbf{S}_{1},\mathbf{u}\rangle\right\rvert^{12}\leq C
. Then using Holder’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}S_{1j}^{3}\right\rvert^{3}|\langle\mathbf{S}_{1},\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\rangle|^{3}\right)\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{j}\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}S_{1j}^{3}\right\rvert^{4}\right)\right\}^{3/4}\left(\mathbb{E}|\langle\mathbf{S}_{1},\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\rangle|^{12}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\|^{3}

for any
\mathbf{u}
. Since
\mathbf{S}_{k}
are i.i.d. samples, using the Berry-Esseen theorem, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}

\displaystyle~\left\lvert\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\leq x\right)-\Phi(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{6}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}S_{1j}^{3}\right\rvert^{3}\dfrac{\left\lvert\langle\mathbf{S}_{1},\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right\rvert^{3}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\dfrac{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\|^{3}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}^{3}}\leq\dfrac{C}{\sqrt{n}}.

(47)

Note that under the event
{\cal A}
defined in (
43
), we have using (
45
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\mathbf{a}_{j})\leq x\right)\in\bigg(

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\bigg(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\leq x-\dfrac{\sqrt{n}\xi}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\bigg),

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\bigg(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\leq x+\dfrac{\sqrt{n}\xi}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\bigg)\bigg).

for some
\xi\in\left(0,\dfrac{Cd(\log d)^{2}}{n}\right)
. Note that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lvert\Phi\left(x+\dfrac{\sqrt{n}\xi}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\right)-\Phi(x)\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\dfrac{\sqrt{n}\xi}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\leq\dfrac{Cd(\log d)^{2}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}},

and therefore, using the bound above, when
\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}\geq c
for some constant
c>0
, we have

\displaystyle~\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}:\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}>c}\left\lvert\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}}\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\mathbf{a}_{j})\leq x\right)-\Phi(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{Cd(\log d)^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\mathbb{P}({\cal A})+\mathbb{P}({\cal A}^{c})\leq\dfrac{Cd(\log d)^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\dfrac{C(\log d)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{d}},

by our assumption that
\mathbb{E}S_{k}^{12}\leq C
, along with
n\geq Cd^{2}(\log d)^{3}
in the last step. The proof is now complete since by assumption we consider
\mathbf{u}
such that there is a constant
c>0
such that
\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}>c
for sufficiently large
d
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4.3
.

The claim follows by considering
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{i},\perp}\mathbf{e}_{j}
in Theorem
4.1
and then applying part i) of Corollary
4.2
. We omit the details for brevity.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4.2
.

We assume without loss of generality that
\pi=Id
and that
\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j},\,\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle>0
for
1\leq j\leq d
. We will use the asymptotic expansion of
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}
from Lemma
17
.

We first derive a simpler expression for
\mathbf{A}_{j,\perp}^{\top}\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}
. Let
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
are the vectors obtained by removing the
j^{\text{th}}
element of
\mathbf{S}
. The vectors
\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\prime}
are derived from
\mathbf{S}_{k}
similarly, for
k=1,\dots,n
. By the independence of
S_{l}
for
1\leq l\leq d
, we have

\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{3}\mathbb{E}\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathbf{0}.

Then by the definition of
\mathscr{E}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}_{j}:=\mathbf{A}_{j,\perp}^{\top}\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}=

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{j,\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{kj}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{k}-\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1}\right)=\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{kj}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\prime}=\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{kj}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\prime}.

It can be checked that

\bm{\Sigma}:={\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{1})=\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{6}\mathbb{I}_{d-1}.

Case i: Concentration bound

In general, we can follow the proof of Lemma
15
, using the vector Bernstein inequality from
Minsker 2017
, to get that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Y}_{j}\|\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d(\log(1/\delta))}{n}}

with probability at least
1-\delta-d^{-3}-n^{-\epsilon/8}
.

Case ii:
n\geq d^{3}(\log d)^{2}
:

We now derive the asymptotic distribution of
1-\langle\mathbf{a}_{j},\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}\rangle^{2}
, in the case where
n\geq Cd^{3}(\log d)^{2}
. Using the Berry-Esseen theorem for hyper-rectangles, for a set of independent random variables
Z_{k}\sim N(0,1)
, for
k=1,\dots,d-1
, we apply part 2 of Proposition 2 from
Chernozhukov et al. 2017
to obtain

\sup_{x_{1},\dots,x_{d-1}>0}\left\lvert\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}|Y_{jk}|}{\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{6}}\leq x_{k}\text{ for all }k\neq j\right)-\mathbb{P}(|Z_{k}|\leq x_{k}\text{ for all }k\neq j)\right\rvert\leq\max_{k\neq j}\dfrac{Cd^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{12}\mathbb{E}S_{1k}^{4}}{\sqrt{n}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{y\geq 0}\left\lvert\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{n\mathbf{Y}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{j}}{(\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{6})^{2}}\leq y\right)-\mathbb{P}(\chi^{2}_{d}\leq y)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup_{y\geq 0}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{y}\int_{0}^{y-x_{1}}\dots\int_{0}^{y-x_{1}-\dots-x_{d-2}}\left[d\mu_{n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})-f_{Z^{2}}(x_{1})\dots f_{Z^{2}}(x_{d-1})dx_{1}\dots d_{x_{d-1}}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\max_{k\neq j}\dfrac{Cd^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{12}\mathbb{E}S_{1k}^{4}}{\sqrt{n}}.

In the above we write
d\mu_{n}
to denote the law of
(nY_{j1}^{2},\dots,nY_{j(d-1)}^{2})
. The last line uses the difference in hyper-rectangle probabilities derived earlier. Plugging in the expression for
\mathbf{Y}_{j}
into part i) of Lemma
17
finishes the proof in the case
n\geq Cd^{3}(\log d)^{2}
. ∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4
.

The conclusion follows by retracing the steps of Theorem
4.1
, by using the expression from Lemma
17
for each linear form, and then using multivariate Berry-Esseen theorem for hyperrectangles, i.e., from part 2 of Proposition 2 from
Chernozhukov et al. 2017
. We derive the covariance matrix since it is the only difference.

Using the expression from the second part of Lemma
17
, it is enough to compute the asymptotic covariance

\Cov\left(\sqrt{n}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\top}\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{i}/\kappa_{4}(S_{i}),\sqrt{n}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\top}\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}/\kappa_{4}(S_{j})\right).

Since

\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{i}=\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{li}^{3}\mathbf{X}_{l}-\mathbb{E}(S_{l}^{3}\mathbf{X})=\mathbf{A}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{li}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{l}-\mathbb{E}(S_{l}^{3}\mathbf{S})\right).

Since
\mathbf{S}_{l}
are independent samples for
l=1,\dots,n
, it is enough to compute the asymptotic covariance (when
i\neq j
) which are given by

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{4}(S_{i})\kappa_{4}(S_{j})}\Cov(S_{i}^{3}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S},S_{j}^{3}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{4}(S_{i})\kappa_{4}(S_{j})}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\Cov(S_{i}^{3}\mathbf{S},S_{j}^{3}\mathbf{S})\mathbf{A}\mathbf{u}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{4}(S_{i})\kappa_{4}(S_{j})}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}(\mathbb{E}S_{i}^{4}\mathbb{E}S_{j}^{4}\mathbf{e}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top})\mathbf{A}\mathbf{u}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{4}(S_{i})\kappa_{4}(S_{j})}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{a}_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{a}_{i}\mathbb{E}S_{i}^{4}\mathbb{E}S_{j}^{4}.

The variances remain exactly same as Theorem
4.1
, and this finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.5
.

We define the matrix
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}=\left[\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{1}\,\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{2}\,\dots\,\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{d}\right]
. Then for any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\right)\mathbf{v}=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j}v_{j}\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\mathbf{a}_{j})=\sum_{j}v_{j}\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\left(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle-1\right)+\sum_{j}v_{j}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}.

By part i) of Lemma
17
, we have under the event
{\cal A}
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert\sum_{j}v_{j}\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\left(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle-1\right)\right\rvert\leq

\displaystyle~\max_{j}\left(1-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\right)\leq\max_{j}\left(1-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{\pi(j)},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}{\kappa_{j}^{2}}+\left(\dfrac{Cd(\log d)}{n}\right)^{3/2}\leq\dfrac{Cd(\log d)}{n}.

Similarly, by part ii) of Lemma
17
, under the event
{\cal A}
defined in (
43
) we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert\sum_{j}v_{j}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j}-\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{Cd^{3/2}(\log d)}{n}.

This step uses the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality on the remainder and may be sub-optimal. By the last two steps, we therefore have

\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A})\mathbf{v}-\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{Cd^{3/2}(\log d)}{n}.

(48)

It remains to use the randomness of
\mathscr{E}
and derive the asymptotic distribution. We remind the reader that

\mathscr{E}=\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{X}_{k}^{\circ 4}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\circ 4}.

Moreover
\mathbf{X}_{k}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,n
. Thus

\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}=~\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{A}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{kj}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{k}-\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1}\right).

(49)

Since
\mathbf{S}_{k}
have independent components
S_{kj}
for
k=1,\dots,n
and
j=1,\dots,d
, we obtain

{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1})={\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3})\cdot\mathbb{I}_{d}+({\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{4})-{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3}))\mathbf{e}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top},

and for
j_{1}\neq j_{2}
,

{\rm Cov}(S_{1j_{1}}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1},S_{1j_{2}}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1})=\mathbb{E}(S_{1j_{1}}^{4})\mathbb{E}(S_{1j_{2}}^{4})\mathbf{e}_{j_{2}}\mathbf{e}_{j_{1}}^{\top}.

Therefore

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\left(\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{A}S_{1j}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}^{2}{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3})}{\kappa_{j}^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}+\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\dfrac{v_{j_{1}}v_{j_{2}}\mathbb{E}(S_{1j_{1}}^{4})\mathbb{E}(S_{1j_{2}}^{4})}{\kappa_{j_{1}}\kappa_{j_{2}}}\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j_{1}},\perp}\mathbf{e}_{j_{2}}\mathbf{e}_{j_{1}}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j_{2}},\perp}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}^{2}{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3})}{\kappa_{j}^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{D}_{\mathbf{v}}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{u}

where
\mathbf{D}_{\mathbf{v}}:={\rm diag}\left(\displaystyle\sum_{j\neq 1}\dfrac{v_{j}^{2}{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3})}{\kappa_{j}^{2}}\,\,\sum_{j\neq 2}\dfrac{v_{j}^{2}{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3})}{\kappa_{j}^{2}}\,\,\dots\,\,\sum_{j\neq d}\dfrac{v_{j}^{2}{\rm Var}(S_{1j}^{3})}{\kappa_{j}^{2}}\right)
is a diagonal matrix.

Since
\mathbf{S}_{k}
are i.i.d. samples, we have from (
49
) that for fixed
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}
s.t.

\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}^{2}:=\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{D}_{\mathbf{v}}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{u}\geq c

(50)

we have, using the univariate Berry-Esseen theorem, that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lvert\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\leq x\right)-\Phi(x)\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{6}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}S_{1j}^{3}\right\rvert^{3}|\langle\mathbf{S}_{1},\mathbf{u}\rangle|^{3}\right).

(51)

Since we assume that
\mathbb{E}|S_{1j}|^{12}\leq C
, it follows by the independence of
S_{1j}
that

\sup_{\mathbf{v}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}S_{1j}^{3}\right\rvert^{4}\right)\leq C.

Similarly it follows that for any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, that

\mathbb{E}|\langle\mathbf{S}_{1},\mathbf{u}\rangle|^{12}\leq C

using Lemma
19
. Then by Holder’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}S_{1j}^{3}\right\rvert^{3}\|\mathbf{S}_{1}\|^{3}\right)\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{v}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}S_{1j}^{3}\right\rvert^{4}\right)\right\}^{3/4}\left(\mathbb{E}|\langle\mathbf{S}_{1},\mathbf{u}\rangle|^{12}\right)^{1/4}\leq C.

It therefore follows from (
51
) that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\sum_{j}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\leq x\right)-\Phi(x)\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{C}{\sqrt{n}}.

(52)

Note that under the event
{\cal A}
defined in (
43
), we have using (
48
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A})\mathbf{v}\leq x\right)\in\bigg(

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sum_{j}\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\leq x-\dfrac{\sqrt{n}\xi}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\bigg),

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sum_{j}\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\dfrac{v_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\cdot\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{a}_{j},\perp}\mathbf{u}\right)\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\leq x+\dfrac{\sqrt{n}\xi}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\bigg)\bigg).

for some
\xi\in\left(0,\dfrac{Cd^{3/2}(\log d)^{2}}{n}\right)
. Note that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lvert\Phi\left(x+\dfrac{\sqrt{n}\xi}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\right)-\Phi(x)\right\rvert\leq\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\dfrac{\sqrt{n}\xi}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\leq\dfrac{Cd^{3/2}(\log d)^{2}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}},

and therefore, using the bound from (
52
), we get that, when
\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}\geq c
for some constant
c>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}:\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}>c}\left\lvert\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}}\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A})\mathbf{v}\leq x\right)-\Phi(x)\right\rvert\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{Cd^{3/2}(\log d)}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\mathbb{P}({\cal A})+\mathbb{P}({\cal A}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{Cd^{3/2}(\log d)}{\sqrt{n}}+C\dfrac{(\log d)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{d}}

by our assumption that
\mathbb{E}|S_{kj}|^{12}\leq C
. The proof is now complete since by assumption we consider
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}
such that there is a constant
c>0
such that
\sigma_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}>c
for sufficiently large
d
.
∎

A.4
Proofs of Lemmas

Proof of Lemma
1
.

We will use the low-degree polynomial method with a basis chosen as follows.
\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
are
n
independent copies of
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}
, an observation from the ICA model. For
1\leq k\leq d
we take the source random variables
S_{k}
to be independent copies of

S=RE/\sqrt{2}

where
R
and
E
are Rademacher and
\chi^{2}_{1}
random variables respectively. Fixing two matrices
\mathbf{P},\mathbf{Q}\in{\cal O}(d)
, we compute the low-degree projection of the likelihood ratio as follows. Writing
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}=(\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n})
, we have

\Lambda_{\leq D}^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{N}\langle L,\psi_{t}\rangle^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\otimes n}}\left(L(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\psi_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\right)\right)^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{\otimes n}}\left(\psi_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\right)\right)^{2}.

(53)

Let

\mathbf{Z}=(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{Z}_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d},

be a matrix of i.i.d. standard Gaussian elements
Z_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}N(0,1)
. We consider the polynomial basis

h_{m}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}/\sqrt{d})\quad\text{ for }m\in[D],\,\,j\in[d],\,\,\text{and}\,\,i\in[n].

Here
h_{m}
is the
m^{\text{th}}
probabilist’s Hermite polynomial. We define

\psi_{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{d}}^{(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\left({h_{\alpha_{i,j}}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}/\sqrt{d})}-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}{h_{\alpha_{i,j}}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}/\sqrt{d})}\right)

for
1\leq\alpha_{i,j}\leq D

and

1\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{k}\leq d
.
Note that we have orthonormal matrices
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
. It is immediate that
\|\mathbf{P}\mathbf{z}_{j}\|_{2}=\|\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{z}_{j}\|_{2}
for all
1\leq j\leq d
. Moreover since the odd moments of
S
are zero, it can be checked that
under both the measures
\mu
and
\nu
induced by
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
respectively, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\eta}h_{2}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{X}/\sqrt{d})=\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\psi_{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n,d}}^{(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})=0\,\,\text{ for }\sum_{l=1}^{k}\alpha_{l}=2r+1;\,\,\eta\in\{\mu,\nu\}

and

j,j_{1},\dots,j_{k}\in[d],k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Thus all vectors
\undertilde{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n,d})
such that
\alpha_{i,j}=2
or
\alpha_{i,j}=2k+1
, contributes zero to the value
\Lambda
. We now bound the even powers contributing to
\Lambda_{\leq D}
. Let
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}
be independent copies of
\mathbf{S}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
. We will compute

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}}\Lambda_{\leq D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}}\sum_{\widetilde{\alpha}\in[D]^{n\times d},\widetilde{j}\in[d]^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{j}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}_{i},\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{j}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{j}}\prod_{i:\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4}\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

(54)

We next use the following lemma.

Lemma 7
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{j}}\prod_{i:\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4}\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left(\dfrac{C\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{4}}{d}\right)^{\|\alpha\|_{0}}\left(\dfrac{(\log(d))^{3}}{d}\right)^{\|\alpha\|_{1}/2}.

where
\|\bm{\alpha}_{0}\|=\sum_{i,j}\mathbbm{1}(\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4)
and
\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{1}=\sum_{i,j}\alpha_{i,j}\mathbbm{1}(\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4)
.

Using (
A.4
) and the Lemma above, we then have,

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}}\Lambda_{\leq D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{s=4}^{D}\sum_{m=1}^{s/4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\\
\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{0}=m\\
\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{1}=s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\prod_{i:\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4}\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{s=4}^{D}\sum_{m=1}^{s/4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\\
\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{0}=m\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{1}=s\end{subarray}}\left(\dfrac{C\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{4}}{d}\right)^{m}\left(\dfrac{(\log(d)^{3})^{2}}{d}\right)^{s/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{s=4}^{D}\sum_{m=1}^{s/4}{{nd}\choose m}m^{s/2}\left(\dfrac{C\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{4}}{d}\right)^{m}\left(\dfrac{(\log(d))^{3}}{d}\right)^{s/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{s=4}^{D}\left(\dfrac{s(\log(d))^{3}}{4d}\right)^{s/2}\sum_{m=1}^{s/4}\left(\dfrac{Cnd\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{4}}{d}\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{s=4}^{D}\left(\dfrac{s(\log(d))^{3}}{4d}\right)^{s/2}\left(Cn\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{4}\right)^{s/4}
