\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{s=4}^{D}\left(\dfrac{Cs^{2}(\log d)^{10}n\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{16d^{2}}\right)^{s/4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{s=4}^{D}\left(\dfrac{CD^{2}(\log d)^{10}n\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{d^{2}}\right)^{s/4}\leq~1+\dfrac{CD^{2}(\log d)^{10}n\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{d^{2}}

if
n\leq d^{2}/\left(CD^{2}\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{10}\right)
for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
. To finish the proof we show how to construct
\mathbf{Q}
from
\mathbf{P}
. For any
\mathbf{P}\in{\cal O}(d)
, we take

\mathbf{q}_{1}=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathbf{p}_{1}+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathbf{p}_{2};\,\,\mathbf{q}_{2}=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathbf{p}_{1}-\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathbf{p}_{2};\,\,\mathbf{q}_{k}=\mathbf{p}_{k}\,\,\text{for }3\leq k\leq d.

This implies
\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=2(\sqrt{2}-1)
but
\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sin\angle\left(\mathbf{p}_{k},\mathbf{q}_{k}\right)=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
. Thus

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}}\Lambda_{\leq D}\leq 1+\varepsilon\quad\text{when }n\leq\dfrac{d^{2}\varepsilon}{CD^{2}(\log d)^{10}}

for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2
.

We will use Lemma
12
as an intermediate step. Let us define
\mathbf{Y}_{i}=\mathbf{S}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{i}
and
\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{S}\otimes\mathbf{S}
. We also define
\bm{\alpha}:=\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{Y})={\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})
. Then, since
\mathbf{A}
is orthonormal, we have

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{X}_{l}^{\circ 4}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\circ 4}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{S}_{l},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert(\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v})^{\top}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}_{l}\mathbf{Y}_{l}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\right)(\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert(\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v})^{\top}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}-\bm{\alpha}\bm{\alpha}^{\top})\right)(\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v})\right\rvert

\displaystyle~+2\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert(\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v})^{\top}\alpha\right\rvert\left\lvert\left\langle\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha},\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}+2\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert\mathbf{v}^{\top}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{S}_{l}\mathbf{S}_{l}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{S}^{\top})\right)\mathbf{v}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}+2\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{S}_{l}\mathbf{S}_{l}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{S}^{\top})\right\rVert.

(55)

with probability at least
1-\exp(-Cn^{\epsilon/8})-\exp(-Ct_{1})-\dfrac{1}{Cn^{\epsilon/8}t_{2}^{2+\epsilon/4}}
. Here we use Lemma
12
to bound the first term, and the fact that
\bm{\alpha}={\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})
to get that for all
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\left\lvert(\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v})^{\top}\bm{\alpha}\right\rvert=\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}=\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{S}^{\top})\mathbf{v}=1.

Finally note that
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{S}=0
. Then applying a truncation technique similar to the proofs of Lemmas
12
,
14
and
15
we obtain

\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{S}_{l}\mathbf{S}_{l}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{S}^{\top})\right\rVert\leq C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d}{n}}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-Cn^{\epsilon/8})-\exp(-Ct_{1})-\dfrac{1}{Cn^{\epsilon/8}t_{2}^{2+\epsilon/4}}
. Plugging in this bound into (
A.4
) finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3
.

We define
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}=\mathbf{S}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}=\mathbf{S}\otimes\mathbf{S}
. Moreover, let
\bm{\alpha}:={\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})
. Note first that since
\mathbf{A}
is orthonormal,

(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})\bm{\alpha}={\rm vec}(\mathbf{A}{\rm unvec}(\bm{\alpha})\mathbf{A}^{\top})={\rm vec}(\mathbf{A}(\mathbb{I}_{d})(\mathbf{A})^{\top})={\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})=\bm{\alpha}.

As before, we define
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}:=\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{I}_{d}
. Hence

{\rm vec}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}})={\rm vec}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{I}_{d}\right)=(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})({\rm vec}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}})-\bm{\alpha}).

Since
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}
for an orthonormal matrix
\mathbf{A}
, it follows by the above equations that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{M}}=

\displaystyle~(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\right)(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})^{\top}+\bm{\alpha}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}+\bm{\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}+(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\bm{\alpha})\bm{\alpha}^{\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\right)(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-\bm{\alpha}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}-(\bar{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}}-\bm{\alpha})(\bar{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\right)(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})^{\top}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{(12)(34)}(\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}))=

\displaystyle~(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top})(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})\left(\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\bm{\alpha})(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-\bm{\alpha}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\right)(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})^{\top}.

Thus,

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{M}}-{\cal M}_{(12)(34)}(\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}))\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-(\bar{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}}-\bm{\alpha})(\bar{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bar{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}}-\bm{\alpha}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}

with the required probability. The last step of the proof follows from Lemma
12
, along with the fact that

\left\lVert\bar{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}}-\bm{\alpha}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{S}_{i}\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{I}_{d}\right\rVert_{\rm F}\leq C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}

with the same argument as in Lemma
12
but now used for concentration around the
d\times d
matrix given by
{\rm Var}(\mathbf{S})
.
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4
.

Writing
\mathbf{Y}_{k}=\mathbf{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,n
, and
\mathscr{E}:=\mathcal{M}_{(12)(3,4)}^{-1}(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{1})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})
, we have

\displaystyle~{{\cal M}}_{(12)(34)}(\mathscr{E})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\mathbf{X}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{i})(\mathbf{X}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{i})^{\top}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}){\rm vec}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}})^{\top}-{\rm vec}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}){\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})^{\top}-{{\cal M}}_{(12)(34)}(\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{k}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(\mathbf{Y}_{k}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top}+{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}){\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{1}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{k}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(\mathbf{Y}_{k}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top}+{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}){\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{1}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{k}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(\mathbf{Y}_{k}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left((\mathbf{Y}_{1}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(\mathbf{Y}_{1}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top}\right).

In the last two steps we use the fact that
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{k}={\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})
. We define

\widehat{\mathbf{P}}:={\rm Proj}_{d}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{2}-{\cal M}_{(12)(3,4)}(\mathscr{M}_{0})\right).

which is independent of
\mathscr{E}
. Let us also define the true projecion

{\mathbf{P}}:={\rm Proj}_{d}\left({\cal M}_{(12)(3,4)}(\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0})\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{d}(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})^{\top}.

By Lemma
3
and the Davis-Kahan theorem it follows that

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P}\right\rVert\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}

with high probability. Note that our estimator can be written as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{M}}=

\displaystyle~(\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0}+\mathscr{E})\times_{(3,4)}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0}+(\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{(3,4)}(\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P})+\mathscr{E}\times_{(3,4)}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}

which implies

\displaystyle~{\cal M}_{(1)(234)}(\widehat{\mathscr{M}}-(\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{0}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes((\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})))^{\top}+{\cal M}_{(1)(234)}(\mathscr{E}\times_{(3,4)}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}).

Note that the first matrix on the right hand side above can be bounded as

\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\mathbf{a}_{k}(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes((\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P})(\mathbf{a}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{k})))^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\kappa_{4}(S_{k})\cdot\|\widehat{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P}\|\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}

with high probability. We thus bound the second term

\displaystyle~\left\lVert{\cal M}_{(1)(234)}(\mathscr{E}\times_{(3,4)}\widehat{\mathbf{P}})\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{3}-1}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}u_{i}v_{jkl}(((\mathbb{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E})(\mathbf{Y}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(\mathbf{Y}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top})\widehat{\mathbf{P}})_{(i,j)(k,l)}.

where
\mathbb{E}_{n}
is the sample averaging operator over
n/2
i.id. samples. Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{m}
be independent copies of
\mathbf{X}
for
m=1,\dots,n
that are also independent of
\mathbf{X}_{m}
. Note first that
\widehat{\mathbf{P}}
is independent of
\mathbf{Y}_{i}
s. Then by a decoupling argument, we have for a constant
C>0
that

\displaystyle~\left\lVert{{\cal M}}_{(1)(234)}(\mathscr{E}\times_{(3,4)}\widehat{\mathbf{P}})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{3}-1}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}u_{i}v_{jkl}((\mathbb{E}_{n}(\mathbf{Y}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top})\widehat{\mathbf{P}})_{(i,j)(k,l)}.

with high probability. We now compute the norm on the right. Note that we have

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}u_{i}v_{jkl}(((\mathbb{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E})(\mathbf{Y}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(\mathbf{Y}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top})\widehat{\mathbf{P}})_{(i,j)(k,l)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg\langle\mathbf{v}_{j},\bigg\{\dfrac{2}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n/2}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt~-\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top})\bigg\}(\mathbb{I}_{d}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{j})\mathbf{u}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\mathbf{v}_{j}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\times

\displaystyle~\quad\times\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg\|\bigg\{\dfrac{2}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n/2}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt~-\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top})\bigg\}(\mathbb{I}_{d}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{j})\mathbf{u}\bigg\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg\|\bigg\{\dfrac{2}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n/2}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(S_{jm}\mathbf{S}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(S_{jm}\mathbf{S}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top})\bigg\}\bigg\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

(56)

Here we define
\mathbf{v}_{j}:=(\mathbb{I}_{d}\otimes\mathbf{a}_{j}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{d})\mathbf{v}
for
j=1,\dots,d
. Conditional on
\widehat{\mathbf{P}}
we now apply truncated matrix Bernstein inequality (following the arguments of Lemmas
14
-
15
) to obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg\{\dfrac{2}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n/2}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(S_{jm}\mathbf{S}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(S_{jm}\mathbf{S}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top})\bigg\}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(t_{1}+t_{2})\bigg\{\sqrt{\dfrac{{\rm trace}(\widehat{\mathbf{P}}{\rm Var}(\mathbf{Y}))\left\lVert{\rm Var}(S_{j}\mathbf{S})\right\rVert}{n}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{{\rm trace}({\rm Var}(S_{j}\mathbf{S}))\left\lVert{\rm Var}(\mathbf{Y})\right\rVert}{n}}\bigg\}

(57)

with probability at least
1-n^{-C\varepsilon/8}-\exp(-Ct_{1})-\dfrac{1}{Cn^{\varepsilon/8}t_{2}^{2+\varepsilon/4}}
. To see this, we first truncate the observations according to
{\cal S}(t):=\{m\in[n/2]:\|\mathbf{S}_{m}\|\leq t\}
, and then choose
t
such that
|[n/2]/{\cal S}(t)|\leq C
with high probability. On
m\in{\cal S}(t)
, we apply matrix Bernstein inequality. See the proofs of Lemmas
14
-
15
for the details and more of the similar arguments. Note that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{P}}=

\displaystyle~{\rm Proj}_{d}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}_{m}\mathbf{Y}_{m}^{\top}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top}-(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})){\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\rm Proj}_{d}\left((\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}(\mathbf{S}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{m})(\mathbf{S}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{m})^{\top}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\top}-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})^{\top}\right)(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A}){\rm Proj}_{d}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}(\mathbf{S}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{m})(\mathbf{S}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{m})^{\top}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})(\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}})^{\top}-(\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}){\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})^{\top}\right)(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})^{\top}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{A})^{\top}.

where
{\rm Proj}_{d}
refers to the projection to the top
d
singular vectors, and
\widetilde{\Gamma}=\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n}(\mathbf{S}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{m})-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})
. This implies that

{\rm trace}(\widehat{\mathbf{P}}{\rm Var}({\mathbf{Y}}))={\rm trace}(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}{\rm Var}(\mathbf{S}\otimes\mathbf{S}))
. We compute

\displaystyle({\rm Var}(\mathbf{S}\otimes\mathbf{S}))_{ijkl}={\rm Cov}(S_{i}S_{j},S_{k}S_{l})=\begin{cases}{\rm Var}(S_{i}^{2})\quad&\text{if }i=j=k=l\\
1\quad&\text{if }{\rm card}(\{i,j,k,l\})=2\\
0\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

It can be checked by a direct calculation that this implies

\displaystyle~{\rm trace}(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}{\rm Var}(\mathbf{S}\otimes\mathbf{S}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\max{\rm Var}(S_{i}^{2}){\rm trace}(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}})+\sum_{i,j}(\mathbf{e}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\top}+\sum_{i}(\mathbf{e}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\sum_{j}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~d\max{\rm Var}(S_{i}^{2})+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{i,j}u_{k,ij}u_{k,ji}+\left\lVert\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\right\rVert\times\left\lVert\sum_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~d\max{\rm Var}(S_{i}^{2})+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{i,j}u_{k,ij}^{2}+d

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Cd.

In the second inequality we use an SVD of
\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
where
\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times d}
is an orthonormal matrix. We also use the fact that

{\rm trace}(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}})={\rm rank}(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}})=d
and that
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\|=1
. Plugging this bound into (
A.4
) we obtain

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{Y}_{m}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(S_{jm}\mathbf{S}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d}{n}\left(\left\lVert{\rm Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\right\rVert+C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}\right)}\leq C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d}{n}}.

In the second inequality we use Lemma
12
. The third inequality uses the fact that
\|{\rm Var}(\mathbf{Y})\|\leq C
and
n\geq Cd^{2}
. Finally plugging this bound into (
A.4
) and (
A.4
) we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}\sup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}u_{i}v_{jkl}(((\mathbb{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E})(\mathbf{Y}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))(\mathbf{Y}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))^{\top})\widehat{\mathbf{P}})_{(i,j)(k,l)}\leq

\displaystyle~C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}.

with probability at least
1-n^{-C\varepsilon/8}-\exp(-Ct_{1})-\dfrac{1}{Cn^{\varepsilon/8}t_{2}^{2+\varepsilon/4}}
. This finishes the proof for
{\cal M}_{(1)(234)}(\mathscr{E})
. The proof for
{\cal M}_{(2)(134)}
follows by an analogous argument by swapping the indices
i
and
j
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5
.

The proof follows from Lemmas
2
,
14
and
15
using the definitions of
\Delta_{k}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that

1-(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{q}_{1})^{2}=\max_{1\leq k\leq d}1-(\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{q}_{k})^{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}.

Then we prove the statement for
j=1
. We define

\mathbf{v}=\widehat{\mathbf{a}}/\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|.

By assumption on
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}
, and since
\mathbf{a}_{1}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we can show that

\left\lvert 1-\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|\right\rvert\leq\eta_{1},\quad\text{and}\quad|\langle\mathbf{a}_{1},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\geq\dfrac{1-\eta_{1}}{\sqrt{1+\eta_{1}}}.

Note that
\mathbf{X}_{n}
is independent of
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}
. Thus we first take the expectation over
\mathbf{X}_{n}
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{Q}}f_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}|\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n-1})=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{n})^{4}-\mathbb{E}(\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|\mathbf{p}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{n})^{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{d}((\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{v})^{4}-(\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{p}_{1})^{4})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{4}\left((\mathbf{q}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{v})^{4}-(\mathbf{q}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{p}_{1})^{4}\right)-\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{4}(1-(\mathbf{q}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{p}_{1})^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{4}\left((\mathbf{q}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{v})^{4}-1\right)+2\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{4}(1-(\mathbf{q}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{p}_{1})^{2})(\mathbf{q}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{p}_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{4}\left((\mathbf{q}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{v})^{4}-\dfrac{1}{2}\right).

Since
\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}-\mathbf{q}_{1}\|\leq\eta_{1}\right)\geq 1-\eta_{2}
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{Q}}f(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\geq\kappa_{4}(S_{1})(1-\eta_{1})^{4}\left(\dfrac{(1-\eta_{1})^{4}}{(1+\eta_{1})^{2}}-\eta_{2}/2\right).

(58)

To write the denominator, note that for
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\left((\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{S})^{4}-S_{1}^{4})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{S})^{8}+\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{8}-2\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{S})^{4}S_{1}^{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\kappa_{8}(S_{1})\sum u_{i}^{8}+\sum_{k=0}^{7}{7\choose k}\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{S})^{7-k}\kappa_{k+1}(S_{1})\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{k+1}+\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{8}-2\left(\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{8}u_{1}^{4}+(\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{4})^{2}\sum_{j\neq 1}u_{j}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle~-6\left(\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{6}u_{1}^{2}(1-u_{1}^{2})+\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{4}\sum_{i\geq 2}u_{i}^{2}(1-u_{1}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~7\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{S})^{6}+35\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{S})^{4}\sum u_{i}^{4}+21\kappa_{6}(S_{1})\sum u_{i}^{6}+\kappa_{8}(S_{1})\sum u_{i}^{8}

\displaystyle~+\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{8}-2\left(\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{8}u_{1}^{4}+(\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{4})^{2}\sum_{j\neq 1}u_{j}^{4}\right)-6\left(\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{6}u_{1}^{2}(1-u_{1}^{2})+\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{4}\sum_{i\geq 2}u_{i}^{2}(1-u_{1}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{6}(1-u_{1})

for a numerical constant
C>0
. Then, conditional on
\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n-1}
we can write that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left((\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S})^{4}-S_{1}^{4}\right)\leq~(C\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{6})^{2}\mathbb{E}(1-\mathbf{p}_{1}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{v}})=(C\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{6})^{2}\left(1-\dfrac{1-\eta_{1}}{\sqrt{1+\eta_{1}}}+\eta_{2}\right)

since
\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}-\mathbf{p}_{1}\|\leq\eta_{1})\geq 1-\eta_{2}
when
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{P}
. Dividing equation (
58
) by the expression above, we have

\dfrac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}}f(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}f^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})}}\geq\dfrac{\kappa_{4}(S_{1})(1-\eta_{1})^{4}\left(\dfrac{(1-\eta_{1})^{4}}{(1+\eta_{1})^{2}}-\eta_{2}/2\right)}{C\mathbb{E}S_{1}^{6}\left(1-\dfrac{1-\eta_{1}}{\sqrt{1+\eta_{1}}}+\eta_{2}\right)}\geq 2

provided
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
are sufficiently small.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7
.

Writing
\mathbf{u}_{i}=(\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q})\mathbf{S}_{i}/\sqrt{d}
and
\mathbf{v}_{i}=(\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q})\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}/\sqrt{d}
, we have using Hanson-Wright inequality conditional on
\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{u}_{i}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\geq C\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{v}(\log d))\leq d^{-C}

for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
. Note next that

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}=\dfrac{1}{d}\mathbf{S}^{\top}(\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{d}(\mathbf{S}^{\top}(\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\mathbf{S})^{1/2}(\mathbf{S}^{\prime\top}(\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\mathbf{S}^{\prime})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{d}({\rm tr}(\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}+C\|(\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q})^{2}\|_{\rm F}(\log d))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{C\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)}{d}

with probability at least
1-d^{-C}
, once again using Hanson-Wright inequality and the sub-Gaussianity of
S_{i}
. Combining this bound with the Gaussian quadratic form above we get

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}|\geq C\sqrt{d};\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{u}_{i}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{j}\geq\dfrac{C\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{2}}{d}\right)\leq d^{-C},

(59)

provided
n={\rm poly}(d)
. Now by mean value theorem, and properties of derivatives of Hermite polynomials, we obtain

\displaystyle h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)=~\alpha_{i,j}\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{u}h_{\alpha_{i,j}-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w})

where

\mathbf{w}=(\lambda\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}+(1-\lambda)\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i})/\sqrt{d}

for some
\lambda\in[0,1]
. Similarly writing the term involving
\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle~\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\alpha_{i,j}^{2}\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{j}h_{\alpha_{i,j}-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{i})h_{\alpha_{i,j}-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\prime}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{C\alpha_{i,j}^{2}\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{2}}{d}\cdot h_{\alpha_{i,j}-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{i})h_{\alpha_{i,j}-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\prime}_{i})

(60)

where
\mathbf{w}_{i}=(\lambda\mathbf{P}+(1-\lambda)\mathbf{Q})\mathbf{S}_{i}/\sqrt{d}
and
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}_{i}=(\lambda^{\prime}\mathbf{P}+(1-\lambda^{\prime})\mathbf{Q})\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}/\sqrt{d}
for some
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in[0,1]
. We now derive an upper bound on

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{j}}\prod_{i:\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4}h_{\alpha_{i,j}-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{i})h_{\alpha_{i,j}-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\prime}_{i}).

that holds with high probability over
\mathbf{S}_{i},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}
. For a fixed
j\in[d]
, let the set
{\cal S}_{j}:=\{i:\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4\}
be
{\cal S}_{j}=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{m}\}
. Note that
\mathbf{S}_{i}
and
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}
are independent samples for
i\in{\cal S}_{j}
. On the other hand by properties of Gaussian random variables we can write, for example, that conditional on the
\mathbf{S}_{i}
’s

\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{m}=\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}{\cal P}_{-m}\mathbf{S}_{m}+\|{\cal P}_{-m}^{\perp}\mathbf{S}_{m}\|Z

where
{\cal P}_{-m}
is the projection onto the span of
\{\mathbf{S}_{i},\,\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}:i\in{\cal S}_{j}/i_{m}\}
, and
Z\sim N(0,1)
is independent of
\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{i}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}
for
i\in{\cal S}_{j}
,
i\neq i_{m}
. We compute the above expectation by successively conditioning on
\{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}:i\in\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\}\}
for
k=1,\dots,m-1
. By Lemma
9
, we have

\displaystyle~\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left(h_{{\alpha}_{i_{m},j}-1}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{i_{m}}\right)h_{{\alpha}_{i_{m},j}-1}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\prime}_{i_{m}}\right)\big|\mathbf{S}_{i},\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\prime}:i\in\{i_{1},\dots,i_{m-1}\}\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{\|{\cal P}_{-m}\mathbf{S}_{m}\|}{\sqrt{d}}\max\left\{\dfrac{\|{\cal P}_{-m}\mathbf{S}_{m}\|}{\sqrt{d}},\dfrac{|\mathbf{S}_{m}^{\top}{\cal P}^{\perp}_{-m}\mathbf{S}_{m}|}{\|{\cal P}^{\perp}_{-m}\mathbf{S}_{m}\|\times\|{\cal P}^{\perp}_{-m}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{m}\|}\right\}\right)^{\alpha_{i_{m},j}-1}

\displaystyle\times h_{\alpha_{i_{m},j}-1}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}{\cal P}_{-m}\mathbf{S}_{m}}{\|{\cal P}_{-m}\mathbf{S}_{m}\|}\right)h_{\alpha_{i_{m},j}-1}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}{\cal P}_{-m}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{m}}{\|{\cal P}_{-m}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{m}\|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\left(\dfrac{(\log d)^{5/2}}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i_{m},j}-1}.

(61)

In the last inequality we use the following high probability bounds on
\mathbf{S}_{i},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}
. Note that

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{S}_{m}^{\top}{\cal P}_{-m}\mathbf{S}_{m}\geq{tr}({\cal P}_{-m})+C_{1}\|{\cal P}_{-m}\|_{\rm F}(\log d)\right)\leq d^{-C_{2}}.

for a sufficiently large constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Since
{\cal P}_{-m}
has rank at most
D=C(\log d)
, the bound on the norms follow. Similarly note that

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{S}_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{m}\geq C\sqrt{d}\right)\leq d^{-C}

by the independence of
\mathbf{S}
and
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}
. Thus
\mathbf{S}_{m}^{\top}{\cal P}_{-m}^{\perp}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{m}\leq C\sqrt{d}
with probability at least
1-d^{-C}
. Finally note that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\max_{1\leq k\leq nd}\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{k}\geq C_{1}\sqrt{\log d}\right)\leq d^{-C_{2}}

provided
n={\rm poly}(d)
. Here we write
\mathbf{w}_{k}
to denote unit vectors along
{\cal P}_{-{k}}\mathbf{S}_{k}
and
{\cal P}_{-{k}}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{k}
for
k=2,\dots,m
. Then using upper bounds on Hermite polynomials it follows that, for example,

|h_{\alpha_{i_{m},j}-1}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{k}\right)|\leq|h_{\alpha_{i_{m},j}-1}(C\sqrt{\log d})|\leq C(\log d)^{(\alpha_{i_{m},j}-1)/2}.

The other terms can be bounded similarly. Repeating the argument in (
A.4
) for all
i,j
, along with (
A.4
), we have

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{j}}\prod_{i:\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4}\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{(i,j)\neq(i_{1},1):\alpha_{ij}\geq 4}\dfrac{C\alpha_{i,j}^{2}\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{2}}{d}\left(\dfrac{(\log d)^{5/2}}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}-1}

(62)

Finally we bound the first term in the product as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{1}}\left(h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{1}}h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{1}}h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{1}}h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{1}}h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)h_{\alpha_{i_{1},1}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~2\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\prime}}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i_{1}}}-2\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\prime}}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i_{1}}}.

The equalities follow using part 1 of Lemma
9
and the property that
\mathbb{E}h_{\alpha}^{2}(X)=1
when
X\sim N(0,1)
. We use the lemmas:

Lemma 8
.

\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\prime}}{d}\right)^{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}^{\prime}}{d}\right)^{\alpha}\leq~\dfrac{C\alpha\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{d^{2}}\left(\dfrac{2}{d}\right)^{\alpha/2}

.

Lemma 9
.

For
\rho\in(-1,1)
, any random variable
X
, and
Z\sim N(0,1)
, we have

\mathbb{E}h_{\alpha}(\rho X+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z|X)=\rho^{\alpha}h_{\alpha}(X)

Moreover,

\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha}(\rho_{1}X_{1}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{1})h_{\alpha}(\rho_{2}X_{2}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{2})\big|X_{1},X_{2}\right)\right\rvert\leq C\rho_{1}^{\alpha}\max\{\rho_{2},\rho_{12}\}^{\alpha}h_{\alpha}(X_{1})h_{\alpha}(X_{2})

for a constant
C>0
and
Z_{1},Z_{2}\sim N(0,1)
with
{\rm corr}(Z_{1},Z_{2})=\rho_{12}
.

Plugging in the above bound into (
A.4
) we have

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Z}_{j}}\prod_{i:\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4}\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\left(h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)-h_{\alpha_{i,j}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{i}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{C\alpha_{i_{1},1}\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{d^{2}}\left(\dfrac{2}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i_{1},1}/2}\times\prod_{(i,j)\neq(i_{1},1):\alpha_{ij}\geq 4}\dfrac{C\alpha_{i,j}^{2}\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{2}}{d}\left(\dfrac{(\log d)^{5/2}}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left(\dfrac{C\alpha_{i,j}^{2}\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{2}}{d}\right)^{\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{0}}\prod_{i,j:\alpha_{i,j}\geq 4}\left(\dfrac{(\log d)^{5/2}}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left(\dfrac{C\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}(\log d)^{4}}{d}\right)^{\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{0}}\left(\dfrac{(\log d)^{5/2}}{d}\right)^{\|\bm{\alpha}\|/2}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8
.

Since
S_{j}
are independent random variables with a distribution symmetric around zero, we have

\mathbb{E}(S_{j}/\|\mathbf{S}\|)^{2k-1}=0\quad\text{for all }k\in\mathbb{N}.

By assumption
S_{k}
are independent sub-exponential random variables with
\mathbb{E}S_{k}=0
,
{\rm Var}(S_{k})=1
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|S\|^{2}\leq d/2)\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{d}(S_{k}^{2}-1)\geq d\right)\leq\dfrac{C}{(d/2)^{\alpha}}

by Lemma
18
for
4\alpha
, where
\alpha\leq D
. Thus

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}}\left\{\left(\dfrac{\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\rangle}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}-\left(\dfrac{\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\rangle}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}}\left(\dfrac{1}{d^{\alpha_{i}}}\big[\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\rangle^{\alpha_{i}}-\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\rangle^{\alpha_{i}}\big]\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left(\dfrac{2}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\rangle^{\alpha_{i}}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\rangle^{\alpha_{i}}\right\rvert+\mathbb{P}(\min\{\|\mathbf{S}\|\}\leq d/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left(\dfrac{C}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i}/2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle^{\alpha_{i}}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle^{\alpha_{i}}\right\rvert+C\left(\dfrac{1}{d}\right)^{\alpha_{i}},

(63)

where we write
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}:=\mathbf{S}/\|\mathbf{S}\|
, and use the high probability lower bound on
\|\mathbf{S}\|
. We next expand the moments in terms of cumulants as follows. Writing
\mathbf{O}:=\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\in{\cal O}(d)
, we have:

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle^{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha}\left(B_{\alpha,k}(\kappa_{1}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle),\dots,\kappa_{\alpha-k+1}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle))-B_{\alpha,k}(\kappa_{1}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle),\dots,\kappa_{\alpha-k+1}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle))\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha}\bigg(B_{\alpha,k}(0,1,0,\kappa_{4}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle),\dots,\kappa_{\alpha-k+1}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle))

\displaystyle~\hskip 56.9055pt-B_{\alpha,k}(0,1,0,\kappa_{4}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle),\dots,\kappa_{\alpha-k+1}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle))\bigg)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha}\left(g(\alpha,k,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)-g(\alpha,k,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)\right)

where
B_{\alpha,k}
are incomplete Bell polynomials. Note that the odd cumulants
\kappa_{2k+1}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)=0
and
\kappa_{2k+1}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)=0
since
S_{k}
are symmetric around zero. Moreover
\kappa_{2}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)=\kappa_{2}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)=\sum(S_{i}^{\prime})^{2}/\|\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\|^{2}=1
. Finally we have by the independence of
S_{k}
that

\kappa_{2k}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\kappa_{2k}(S_{i}(S_{u})_{i}^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\kappa_{2k}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\kappa_{2k}(S_{i}\mathbf{o}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}).

The incomplete Bell polynomials can be written as:

\displaystyle B_{\alpha,k}(x_{1},\dots,x_{\alpha-k+1})=\sum\dfrac{\alpha!}{j_{1}!j_{2}!\dots j_{\alpha-k+1}!}\prod_{l=1}^{\alpha-k+1}\left(\dfrac{x_{l}}{l!}\right)^{j_{l}}.

Here the sum is over all
j_{1},\dots,j_{\alpha-k+1}\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{l=1}^{\alpha-k+1}j_{l}=k\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{l=1}^{\alpha-k+1}lj_{l}=\alpha.

Since odd cumulants are zero in our case, the sum only runs over
(0,j_{2},0,j_{4},\dots,j_{2\lfloor(n-k+1)/2\rfloor})
. By the above equations it can then be checked that

g(\alpha,k,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)=g(\alpha,k,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)=0\quad\text{for }k>\alpha/2.

When
k=\alpha/2
, the only possible configuration of
j_{l}
’s for which
g(\cdot)
above takes nonzero values is given by
j_{2}=\alpha/2
and
j_{l}=0
for
l\neq 2
. Thus,

\displaystyle g(\alpha,\alpha/2,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)-g(\alpha,\alpha/2,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{\alpha!}{(\alpha/2)!}\left[\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{2}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2}-\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{2}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2}\right]=~\dfrac{\alpha!}{(\alpha/2)!}\left[\left(\dfrac{1}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2}-\left(\dfrac{1}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2}\right]=0.

Similarly for
k=\alpha/2-1
, the only possible configuration of
j_{l}
’s for which
g(\cdot)
above takes nonzero values is given by
j_{2}=(\alpha-4)/2
,
j_{4}=1
and
j_{l}=0
for
l\neq 2,4
. Thus,

\displaystyle g(\alpha,\alpha/2-1,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)-g(\alpha,\alpha/2-1,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{\alpha!}{(\alpha/2-1)!}\left[\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{2}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2-2}\dfrac{\kappa_{4}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)}{4!}-\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{2}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2-2}\dfrac{\kappa_{4}(\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)}{4!}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{\alpha!}{(\alpha/2-1)!}\left(\dfrac{1}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2-2}\times\dfrac{1}{4!}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\kappa_{4}(S_{i}S^{\prime}_{i}/\|\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\|)-\kappa_{4}(S_{i}\mathbf{o}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}/\|\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\|))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{\alpha!}{(\alpha/2-1)!}\left(\dfrac{1}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2-2}\times\dfrac{1}{4!}\left[\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\sum_{i=1}^{d}\dfrac{(S^{\prime}_{i})^{4}-(\mathbf{o}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime})^{4}}{\|\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\|^{4}}\right]

Thus taking a second expectation over
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}^{\prime}}\left(g(\alpha,\alpha/2-1,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)-g(\alpha,\alpha/2-1,\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{\kappa_{4}(S_{1})\alpha!}{(d/2)^{2}4!(\alpha/2-1)!}\left(\dfrac{1}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2-2}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\{\mathbb{E}(S^{\prime}_{i})^{4}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{o}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime})^{4}\}\right]+C\left(\dfrac{1}{d}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{C\kappa_{4}(S_{1})^{2}\alpha!}{(d/2)^{2}4!(\alpha/2-1)!}\left(\dfrac{1}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2-2}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\{1-\sum_{j=1}^{d}o_{ij}^{4}\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\dfrac{C\kappa_{4}(S_{1})^{2}\alpha!}{(d/2)^{2}4!(\alpha/2-1)!}\left(\dfrac{1}{2!}\right)^{\alpha/2-2}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\{o_{ij}^{2}(1-o_{ij}^{2})\}\right]

where we use the high probability event
\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\|\geq\sqrt{d/2})\geq 1-d^{-\alpha}
in the first inequality. The higher order terms, for
k\leq\alpha/2-2
can be bounded similarly using successive differences in the products of cumulants of orders
(j_{1},\dots,j_{\alpha-k+1})
.

We then have

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle^{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\alpha\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{4}(S_{1})^{2}}{d^{2}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\{o_{ij}^{2}(1-o_{ij}^{2})\}\right]\right)\max\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle^{\alpha},\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S},\mathbf{S}^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{S},\mathbf{O}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}_{u}\rangle^{\alpha}\}+C\left(\dfrac{1}{d}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\alpha\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{4}(S_{1})^{2}}{d^{2}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\{o_{ij}^{2}(1-o_{ij}^{2})\}\right]\right)\left\{1+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{S}^{\prime}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}(\max\{S^{\prime}_{k},\mathbf{o}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\}/\|\mathbf{S}^{\prime}\|)^{\alpha}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\alpha\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{4}(S_{1})^{2}}{d^{2}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\{o_{ij}^{2}(1-o_{ij}^{2})\}\right]\right).

The first inequality follows by comparing the expression for the difference in expectations to the case where
\mathbf{O}=\mathbb{I}_{d}
. To finish the proof, we use the fact that
\mathbf{O}=\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}
and simplify the expression. In particular,

\displaystyle~\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\{o_{ij}^{2}(1-o_{ij}^{2})\}\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{i=1}^{d}(1-o_{ii}^{2})+\sum_{i\neq j}o_{ij}^{2}\leq~2\sum_{i=1}^{d}(1-o_{ii})+\sum_{i\neq j}o_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{i=1}^{d}(2-2\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{q}_{i})+\sum_{i\neq j}(\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q})_{ij}^{2}=~\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|\mathbf{p}_{i}-\mathbf{q}_{i}\|^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j}(\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q})_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|\mathbb{I}_{d}-\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~2\|\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{Q}\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

Plugging this bound into (
A.4
) we have the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9
.

The proof follows equations 82-83 of
Mao and Wein 2021
. It is a standard fact that

h_{\alpha}(x+y)=\sum_{l=0}^{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{l!}{\alpha!}}{\alpha\choose l}x^{\alpha-l}h_{l}(y).

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}h_{\alpha}(\rho X+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{l=0}^{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{l!}{\alpha!}}{\alpha\choose l}\left(\rho X\right)^{\alpha-l}\mathbb{E}h_{l}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{l=0}^{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{l!}{\alpha!}}{\alpha\choose l}\left(\rho X\right)^{\alpha-l}(l-1)!!(l!)^{-1/2}\left((1-\rho^{2})-1\right)^{l/2}\mathbbm{1}(l\text{ is even})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{l=0}^{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{l!}{\alpha!}}{\alpha\choose l}\left(\rho X\right)^{\alpha-l}(l-1)!!(l!)^{-1/2}\left((1-\rho^{2})-1\right)^{l/2}\mathbbm{1}(l\text{ is even})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\rho^{\alpha}\sum_{l=0}^{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{l!}{\alpha!}}{\alpha\choose l}X^{\alpha-l}(l-1)!!(l!)^{-1/2}\mathbbm{1}(l\text{ is even})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\rho^{\alpha}h_{\alpha}(X).

In the third equality, we use the standard fact that

\mathbb{E}h_{l}(\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z)=(l-1)!!(l!)^{-1/2}(-\rho)^{l}\mathbbm{1}(l\text{ is even}).

The second last equality follows by using the third equality for
\rho=1
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}h_{\alpha}(\rho_{1}X_{1}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{1})h_{\alpha}(\rho_{2}X_{2}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{l_{1}=0}^{\alpha}\sum_{l_{2}=0}^{\alpha}\sqrt{\dfrac{l_{1}!l_{2}!}{(\alpha!)^{2}}}{\alpha\choose l_{1}}{\alpha\choose l_{2}}\left(\rho_{1}X_{1}\right)^{\alpha-l_{1}}\left(\rho_{2}X_{2}\right)^{\alpha-l_{2}}\mathbb{E}h_{l_{1}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{1}^{2}}Z_{1}\right)h_{l_{2}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{2}\right)

We can then write
Z_{2}=\rho_{12}Z_{1}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{12}^{2}}Z_{3}
where
Z_{3}
is independent of
Z_{1}
. Then following the previous calculation, we get

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\left(h_{l_{1}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{1}^{2}}Z_{1}\right)h_{l_{2}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}\left(h_{l_{1}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{1}^{2}}Z_{1}\right)h_{l_{2}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{2}\right)|Z_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}h_{l_{1}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{1}^{2}}Z_{1}\right)\mathbb{E}h_{l_{2}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}(\rho_{12}Z_{1}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{12}^{2}}Z_{3})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}h_{l_{1}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{1}^{2}}Z_{1}\right)\mathbb{E}h_{l_{2}}\left(\rho_{12}\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{1}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{12}^{2}-\rho_{2}^{2}+\rho_{2}^{2}\rho_{12}^{2}}Z_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\rho_{12}^{l_{2}}\mathbb{E}h_{l_{1}}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho_{1}^{2}}Z_{1}\right)h_{l_{2}}\left(Z_{1}\right)\leq C\rho_{12}^{l_{2}}\rho_{1}^{l_{1}}

using the expansion of Hermite polynomials in the last step. This implies that

\left\lvert\mathbb{E}h_{\alpha}(\rho_{1}X_{1}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{1})h_{\alpha}(\rho_{2}X_{2}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{2}^{2}}Z_{2})\right\rvert\leq C\rho_{1}^{\alpha}\max\{\rho_{2},\rho_{12}\}^{\alpha}|\mathbb{E}h_{\alpha}(X_{1})h_{\alpha}(X_{2})|.

Here
\rho_{12}
is the correlation between
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 10
.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
be
n
independent copies of
\mathbf{X}
such that
{\cal L}(\mathbf{X})\in{\cal P}_{\rm ICA}(\mathbf{A};\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2})
for some
\mathbf{A}\in{\cal O}(d)
and
M_{1},M_{2}>0
. Let
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{j}
and
\mathbf{A}_{j}
denote the matrix consisting of the first
j
columns of
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathbf{A}
respectively. We then have

\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{j}-\mathbf{A}_{j}\right\rVert\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d(\log d)}{n}}+\dfrac{Cd^{3/2}(\log d)}{n}+C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}\log(\delta)^{2}}{n}}\mathbbm{1}(\epsilon<4)

with probability at least
1-d^{-3}-\delta-n^{-\epsilon/8}-n^{-(\epsilon-4)/12}\mathbbm{1}(\epsilon\geq 4)
, provided
n\geq(d\log\delta)^{2}
.

Proof of Lemma
10
.

We assume that
n\geq C(d\log\delta)^{2}
so that we have

\mathscr{E}:=\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(\mathbf{X});\,\,\Delta_{1}:=\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|,\,\,\Delta_{2}:=\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathscr{E}\times_{3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|.

satisfies

\mathbb{P}\left(\Delta_{1}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd(\log(d))}{n}},\,\,\Delta_{2}\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{Cd(\log d)}{n}},\,\,\Delta\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{C(d\log\delta)^{2}}{n}}\right)\geq 1-\delta-d^{-3}-n^{-\epsilon/8}.

(64)

by the concentration bounds from Lemmas
2
-
15
. For any
1\leq j\leq d
, let us define the matrix
\mathbf{E}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times j}

\mathbf{E}:=[\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{1}\,\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{2}\,\dots\,\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}].

By the expression from Lemma
17
, we have that

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{E}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{j}\times(\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\|\mathscr{E}\|)\leq\dfrac{Cd^{3/2}(\log d)}{n}.

The proof follows by the bound on
\|\mathbf{E}_{j}\|\leq\|\mathbf{E}_{d}\|
from Lemma
16
.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Let
\gamma_{j}:={\rm sign}(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle)
. For
\mathscr{E},\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
defined in (
36
), we have that

\sin\angle\left(\mathbf{a}_{j},\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t+1]}-\dfrac{\gamma_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)\leq\dfrac{CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}+L_{t}^{2}(\kappa_{j}+C\|\mathscr{E}\|)}{\kappa_{j}}.

Proof of Lemma
11
.

By the induction hypothesis, we have estimates
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]}
such that

\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{j}-{\rm sign}((\mathbf{a}_{j})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]})\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{2}L_{t}.

To simplify the proof, we assume that
\gamma_{j}:={\rm sign}(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle)=1
. The other cases can be handled by reversing the sign of
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}
accordingly. We have, for any
\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{a}_{j}
that

\langle\mathbf{v},(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}\rangle=\sum_{i\neq j}\kappa_{4}(S_{i})\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{a}_{i}\rangle(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]},\mathbf{a}_{i}\rangle)^{3}+\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{v}\times_{q>1}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]}

(65)

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the first term is

\left\lvert\sum_{i\neq j}\kappa_{i}\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{a}_{i}\rangle(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]},\mathbf{a}_{i}\rangle)^{3}\right\rvert\leq\max_{i\neq j}\{\kappa_{i}|\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{a}_{i}\rangle|\}(\sin\angle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]},\mathbf{a}_{j}\right))^{3}\leq\kappa_{\max}L_{t}^{3}.

(66)

For the second term, note that

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{v}\times_{q>1}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{[t]}=

\displaystyle~\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{v}\times_{q>1}(\mathbf{a}_{j}+\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}-\mathbf{a}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{v}\times_{q>1}\mathbf{a}_{j}+3\sqrt{2}L_{t}\left\lVert\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right\rVert

\displaystyle~+\sum_{A\subset[p]/\{1\},\,|A|\geq 2}\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{v}\times_{q\in A}(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}-\mathbf{a}_{j})\times_{q^{\prime}\notin A}\mathbf{a}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{v}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}+CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}+CL_{t}^{2}\|\mathscr{E}\|.

(67)

Plugging in the upper bounds from (
66
), (
A.4
) into (
65
), yields

\sup_{\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}\langle\mathbf{v},(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}-\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\leq CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}+L_{t}^{2}(L_{t}\kappa_{\max}+C\|\mathscr{E}\|).

(68)

It remains to bound the norm. We have

\displaystyle\|(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}\|\geq

\displaystyle~(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{1}\mathbf{a}_{j}\times_{2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\kappa_{j}(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle^{3}-\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{a}_{j}\times_{2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\kappa_{j}(1-L_{t}^{2})^{3}-\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}-CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}-CL_{t}^{2}\|\mathscr{E}\|

(69)

following the steps of (
66
), (
A.4
) with
\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}
instead of
\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}
.
Dividing (
68
) by (
A.4
), one has

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}\dfrac{\langle\mathbf{v},(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}-\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle}{\|(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}\|}\leq

\displaystyle\dfrac{CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}+L_{t}^{2}(L_{t}\kappa_{\max}+C\|\mathscr{E}\|)}{\kappa_{j}(1-L_{t}^{2})^{3}-\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}-CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}-CL_{t}^{2}\|\mathscr{E}\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\dfrac{CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}+L_{t}^{2}(\kappa_{j}+C\|\mathscr{E}\|)}{\kappa_{j}}

since by assumption,
L_{0}\leq\min\{1/C,\frac{\kappa_{\min}}{2\kappa_{\max}}\}
, and by induction hypothesis it can be verified that,
L_{t}\leq\min\{1/C,\frac{\kappa_{\min}}{2\kappa_{\max}}\}
. Then
by Lemma
2
,

3\mathscr{E}\times_{1,2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}+CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}+CL_{t}^{2}\|\mathscr{E}\|\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}(\log d)^{2}}{n}}\leq\kappa_{j}.

Moreover, by similar calculations, we also have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{a}_{j}}\mathscr{E}\times_{1}\mathbf{v}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\left(\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}-\dfrac{1}{\|(\widehat{\mathscr{T}}-\mathscr{M}_{0})\times_{2,3,4}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t]}\|}\right)\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{C\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}L_{t}+\varepsilon_{1}L_{t}^{2}(\kappa_{j}+C\|\mathscr{E}\|)}{\kappa_{j}^{2}}.

Combining all the bounds we have

\sin\angle\left(\mathbf{a}_{j},\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{j,[t+1]}-\dfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}\mathscr{E}\times_{2,3,4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\right)\leq\dfrac{CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}+L_{t}^{2}(\kappa_{j}+C\|\mathscr{E}\|)}{\kappa_{j}}.

(70)

Thus,

L_{t+1}\leq\dfrac{\varepsilon_{1}}{\kappa_{j}}+\dfrac{CL_{t}\varepsilon_{2}+L_{t}^{2}(\kappa_{j}+C\|\mathscr{E}\|)}{\kappa_{j}}.

∎

Lemma 12
.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
be
n
independent copies of
\mathbf{X}
s.t.
{\cal L}(\mathbf{X})\in{\cal P}_{\rm ICA}(\mathbf{A};\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2})
for some
\mathbf{A}\in{\cal O}(d)
and
\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2}>0
. Let
\bm{\alpha}:={\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})
. Then defining
\mathbf{Y}_{i}:=\mathbf{S}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
, one has for any
t_{1},t_{2}>0
that

\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\right\rVert\geq C(t_{1}+t_{2})\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}

with probability at most

e^{-Cn^{\epsilon/8}}+\exp(-Ct_{1})+\dfrac{1}{Cn^{\epsilon/8}t_{2}^{2+\epsilon/4}}
.

Proof of Lemma
12
.

We use the truncation strategy which is standard in proving concentration bounds of this nature, previously used in
Adamczak et al. 2010
and
Vershynin 2011
, for example. We use the fact that random vectors
\mathbf{S}_{l}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with independent components. Note that
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{l}={\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})=\bm{\alpha}
. Then we divide the empirical deviation as follows:

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{i}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}(\mathbf{S}_{l}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{S}_{l}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{S}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{S}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}\|\leq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{Y}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{1}\|\leq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})\right\rVert

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}\|\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})\right\rVert

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{Y}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{1}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{1}\|\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})\right\rVert.

We will bound each of these terms separately. To bound the first term, we use matrix Bernstein inequality. We define

\mathbf{M}_{l}=(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}\|\leq C(nd^{2})^{1/4}).

Then since
\|\bm{\alpha}\|=\sqrt{d}
, it is immediate that

\|\mathbf{M}_{l}\|=\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}\|\leq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})\leq C\sqrt{nd^{2}}

almost surely. On the other hand, note that

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top})^{2})_{(k,k)(k,k)}=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}(\sum_{i=1}^{d}(S_{i}^{2}-1)^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j}S_{i}^{2}S_{j}^{2})(S_{k}^{2}-1)^{2}\leq Cd^{2}

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top})^{2})_{(k,k)(l,l)}=

\displaystyle~2\mathbb{E}(S_{k}^{2}(S_{k}^{2}-1)S_{l}^{2}(S_{l}^{2}-1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~2(\kappa_{4}(S_{k})+2)(\kappa_{4}(S_{l})+2)\hskip 56.9055pt\text{if }k\neq l

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top})^{2})_{(k,k)(m,n)}=

\displaystyle~0\hskip 176.407pt\text{if }m\neq n,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top})^{2})_{(k,l)(m,n)}=

\displaystyle~Cd^{2}\mathbbm{1}(\{k,l\}=\{m,n\})\,\hskip 71.13188pt\text{if }k\neq l.

Note that the
d
rows, with indices
(k,k)
, contribute to a singular value of at most
Cd^{2}+C\sqrt{d}\leq Cd^{2}
. The other rows each contribute to at most
Cd^{2}
.

By the orthogonality of the rows
\mathbf{e}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{e}_{j}
, it then follows by a direct calculation that

\left\lVert\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top})^{2}\right\rVert\leq Cd^{2}.

Then one has

\|\mathbf{N}_{l}\|=\|\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{M}_{l}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{M}_{l})^{2}\|\leq Cd^{2}.

By matrix Bernstein inequality
(
Vershynin 2018
, see, e.g., Theorem 5.4.1 of )
we then have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{l}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}\|\leq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}\|\leq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Ct_{1}\sqrt{\dfrac{d^{2}}{n}}

(71)

with probability at least
1-\exp(-Ct_{1})
.

It remains to bound the operator norm of the untruncated average. In the remainder of the proof we will assume that
n\geq Cd^{2}
for a large enough
C>0
. This means
nd^{2}\geq Cd^{4}
. By Lemma
18
, we have

p:=\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}\|\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})=\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{S}\|\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/8})=\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{S}\|^{2}-d\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})\leq\dfrac{d^{2+\epsilon/4}}{(nd^{2})^{1+\epsilon/8}}.

Let us define
M=\{1\leq l\leq n:\|\mathbf{S}_{l}\|\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/8}\}
. Since
\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{S}_{l}\|\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/8})
are independent
{\rm Bernoulli}(p)
random variables, we have

\mathbb{E}|M|=np\leq\dfrac{nd^{2+\epsilon/4}}{(nd^{2})^{1+\epsilon/8}}\leq n^{-\epsilon/8}.

Moreover,

\mathbb{P}\left(|M|\geq C\right)\leq\exp(-Cn^{\epsilon/8})

by the Chernoff bound for Bernoulli random variables. We have using Lemma
18
that for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\|\mathbf{S}_{l}\|^{2}\geq d+t)\leq n\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{S}\|^{2}-d\geq t)\leq\dfrac{nd^{2+\epsilon/4}}{Ct^{4+\epsilon/2}}

(72)

Note that
\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}\|=\|\mathbf{S}_{l}\|^{2}
. Thus we can bound the “untruncated average” as

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}(\mathbf{Y}_{k}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}_{k}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}_{l}\|\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})\right\rVert\geq(d/\sqrt{n}+t_{2})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\cdot|M|\max\limits_{1\leq l\leq n}\|\mathbf{S}_{l}\|^{4}\geq(d/\sqrt{n}+t_{2})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}(|M|\geq C)+\mathbb{P}(|M|\leq C,\,\max\limits_{1\leq k\leq n}\|\mathbf{S}_{k}\|^{4}\geq(d+C\sqrt{n}t_{2})^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\exp(-Cn^{\epsilon/8})+n\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{S}\|^{2}-d\geq C\sqrt{n}t_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\exp(-Cn^{\epsilon/8})+\dfrac{nd^{2+{\epsilon/4}}}{Cn^{2+\epsilon/4}t_{2}^{4+\epsilon/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(-Cn^{\epsilon/8})+\dfrac{d^{2+\epsilon/4}}{Cn^{1+\epsilon/4}t_{2}^{4+{\epsilon/2}}}.

(73)

Finally, once again using the fact that
\mathbf{Y}_{1}:=\mathbf{S}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{\alpha})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{Y}\|\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/4})\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle~(\sup_{\mathbf{v}:\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4})^{1/4}(\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{S}\|^{2}-d\geq C(nd^{2})^{1/4}))^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\cdot\dfrac{1}{n^{1+\epsilon/8}}.

(74)

Here we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the first step, and the fact that for a unit vector
\mathbf{v}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4}=

\displaystyle~\sum_{(i,j)=1}^{d^{2}}\mathbb{E}(S_{i}^{4}S_{j}^{4})v_{(i,j)}^{4}+\sum_{(i,j)\neq(k,l)}\mathbb{E}(S_{i}^{2}S_{j}^{2}S_{k}^{2}S_{l}^{2})v_{(i,j)}^{2}v_{(k,l)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\sum_{(i,j)=1}^{d^{2}}v_{(i,j)}^{4}+C\sum_{(i,j)\neq(k,l)}v_{(i,j)}^{2}v_{(k,l)}^{2}\leq C.

Adding equations (
A.4
), (
A.4
) and (
A.4
) finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
be
n
independent copies of a random vector
\mathbf{X}
such that
{\cal L}(\mathbf{X})\in{\cal P}_{\rm ICA}(\mathbf{A};\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2})
for
\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2}>0
. Then for any
t>C\log d
, we have

\max\limits_{1\leq j\leq d}~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\mathbf{X}_{l}\circ\mathbf{X}_{l}\circ\mathbf{X}_{l}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\mathbf{X}\circ\mathbf{X}\circ\mathbf{X}\right\rVert\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{dt}{n}}+\dfrac{Cd^{13/8}t}{n^{7/8}}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-t)-(nd)^{-\tfrac{\epsilon}{8}}
.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

Note that for any
j\in[d]
,

\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle=\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{l}:=S_{lj}.

By our model assumptions,
\{S_{lj}\}
are independent random variables with mean zero, variance one and
\mathbb{E}|S_{lj}|^{8+\varepsilon}\leq C
.

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\mathbf{X}_{l}\circ\mathbf{X}_{l}\circ\mathbf{X}_{l}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle\mathbf{X}\circ\mathbf{X}\circ\mathbf{X}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{lj}\mathbf{S}_{l}\circ\mathbf{S}_{l}\circ\mathbf{S}_{l}-\mathbb{E}S_{j}\mathbf{S}\circ\mathbf{S}\circ\mathbf{S}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{lj}(\mathbf{S}_{l}\circ\mathbf{S}_{l}-(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\circ\mathbf{S}_{l}-\mathbb{E}\lx@sectionsign_{j}(\mathbf{S}\circ\mathbf{S}-\mathbb{I}_{d})\circ\mathbf{S}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathbb{I}_{d}\circ\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{lj}\mathbf{S}-\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)\right\rVert.

We bound these two terms separately. Note that the second term can be bounded using truncated matrix Bernstein inequality (see the proof of Lemma
15
) to obtain

\left\lVert\mathbb{I}_{d}\circ\left(\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{lj}\mathbf{S}-\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)\right\rVert\leq\dfrac{Cdt}{n}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-t)-d^{-1-\epsilon/8}(n)^{-\epsilon/8}
.

For the first term, we use a similar truncation as done in Lemma
12
. We define

\mathbf{M}:=S_{j}(\mathbf{S}\otimes\mathbf{S}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\circ\mathbf{S}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times d}

and let
\mathbf{M}_{l}
be independent copies of
\mathbf{M}
for
l=1,2,\dots,n
. We write

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{lj}(\mathbf{S}_{l}\circ\mathbf{S}_{l}-(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\circ\mathbf{S}_{l}-\mathbb{E}\lx@sectionsign_{j}(\mathbf{S}\circ\mathbf{S}-\mathbb{I}_{d})\circ\mathbf{S}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{lj}(\mathbf{S}_{l}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{l}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\mathbf{S}_{l}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\lx@sectionsign_{j}(\mathbf{S}\otimes\mathbf{S}-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d}))\circ\mathbf{S}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{M}_{l}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{M}_{l}\|\leq Cd^{13/8}n^{1/8})-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{M}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{M}\|\leq Cd^{13/8}n^{1/8})\right\rVert

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{M}_{l}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{M}_{l}\|\geq Cd^{13/8}n^{1/8})\right\rVert

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\mathbf{M}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{M}\|\leq Cd^{13/8}n^{1/8})\right\rVert.

One can check by a direct calculation that

\max\left\{\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\right\rVert,\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right\rVert\right\}\leq Cd.

Then the first term can be bounded by matrix Bernstein inequality as follows. We have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{M}_{l}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{M}_{l}\|\leq Cd^{13/8}n^{1/8})-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{M}\mathbbm{1}(\|\mathbf{M}\|\leq Cd^{13/8}n^{1/8})\right\rVert\leq C\sqrt{\dfrac{dt}{n}}+\dfrac{Cd^{13/8}n^{1/8}t}{n}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-t)
.

For the second term, as before we compute
p=\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{M}\|\geq Cd^{3/4}n^{1/2})
. Note that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\lVert\mathbf{M}\right\rVert^{2}-d^{3}S_{j}^{2}\right\rvert=

\displaystyle\left\lvert|S_{j}|^{2}\|\mathbf{S}\|^{2}\cdot\|(\mathbf{S}\otimes\mathbf{S})-{\rm vec}(\mathbb{I}_{d})\|^{2}-d^{3}S_{j}^{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lvert S_{j}^{2}\left(\sum S_{k}^{2}\right)\left((\sum S_{k}^{2})^{2}+d-2\sum S_{k}^{2}\right)-d^{3}S_{j}^{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle S_{j}^{2}\left\lvert\sum_{k}(S_{k}^{2}-1)\right\rvert^{3}.

It follows by the moment inequalities in Lemma
18
, see also Remark 2 of
Latała 1997
, that

\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\left\lVert\mathbf{M}\right\rVert^{2}-d^{3}S_{j}^{2}\right\rvert^{(4+\epsilon/2)}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\sum_{k}(S_{k}^{2}-1)\right\rvert^{(8+\epsilon)/2}\leq Cd^{4+\epsilon/2}.

Then by Markov’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{M}\|\geq Cd^{3/2}\cdot(nd)^{1/8}+Cn^{1/8}d^{5/8})\leq\dfrac{1}{(dn)^{1+\epsilon/8}}.

Therefore if we define
{{\cal Q}}=\{1\leq l\leq n:\|\mathbf{M}_{l}\|\geq Cd^{3/2}\cdot(nd)^{1/8}+n^{1/8}d^{5/8}\}
, we have

|{\cal Q}|\leq C

with probability at least
1-\exp(-n^{\epsilon/8})
, using Chernoff bounds on Binomial random variables. Moreover, it also follows by a union bound over all possible
l
and
j
that

\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\|\mathbf{M}_{l}\|\leq Cd^{3/2}\cdot(nd)^{1/8}+Cn^{1/8}d^{5/8}

with probability at least
1-(nd)^{-\epsilon/8}
. Thus the second term in the deviation bound satisfies

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{M}_{l}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{M}_{l}\|\geq Cn^{1/8}d^{13/8}+Cn^{1/8}d^{5/8}\right)\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{n}\cdot|{\cal Q}|\cdot\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}|\mathbf{M}_{l}|\leq\dfrac{Cn^{1/8}d^{13/8}}{n}

(75)

with probability at least
1-(nd)^{-\epsilon/8}
. The third term can be bounded by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality through an argument identical to (
A.4
), and we omit it for brevity. Taking a union bound over
j\in[d]
in the first term (note that the other terms have been bounded uniformly in
j
), we finish the proof.
∎

Lemma 14
.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
be
n
independent copies of
\mathbf{X}
s.t.
{\cal L}(\mathbf{X})\in{\cal P}_{\rm ICA}(\mathbf{A};\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2})
for some
\mathbf{A}\in{\cal O}(d)
and
\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2}>0
. We have, for any
t>C\log d
that

\Delta_{2}:=\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|(\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{4}^{\rm sample}(\mathbf{X})-\mathscr{M}_{4}(\mathbf{X}))\times_{3}\mathbf{a}_{j}\times_{4}\mathbf{a}_{j}\|\geq Ct\sqrt{\dfrac{d}{n}}

with probability at most
\exp(-t)+(nd)^{-\epsilon/8}+n^{-\epsilon/2}
.

Proof of Lemma
14
.

Note that for any
j\in[d]
,

\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle=\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{l}:=S_{lj}
.
By our model assumptions,
\{S_{lj}\}
are independent random variables with mean zero, variance one and
\mathbb{E}|S_{lj}|^{8+\epsilon}\leq C
. Let us define the vectors
\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}=S_{lj}\mathbf{S}_{l}
.

By Markov’s inequality, for any
t>d^{-1/2}
,

\mathbb{P}(|S_{lj}|\geq(ndt)^{1/8})\leq\left(ndt\right)^{-(1+\epsilon/8)}.

Since
\{S_{l(j)}:1\leq j\leq d\}
are independent,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\sum_{i\neq j}S_{lj}^{2}(S_{li}^{2}-1)|^{\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{2}}=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}|S_{lj}|^{8+\epsilon}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{i\neq j}(S_{li}^{2}-1)\right\rvert^{\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{2}}\right)\leq~Cd^{\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{4}}.

using (
91
) for
\alpha=(8+\epsilon)
. Thus by Markov inequality, we also have, for any
t>0
, that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{i\neq j}S_{lj}^{2}(S_{li}^{2}-1)\right\rvert\geq t\right)\leq\dfrac{Cd^{(8+\epsilon)/4}}{t^{(8+\epsilon)/2}}.

Thus, for any
t>d^{-1/2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}\|\geq C(ndt)^{1/4}\right)\leq

\displaystyle~d^{-\tfrac{\epsilon}{8}}(nt)^{-(1+\epsilon/8)}+\dfrac{d}{(nt)^{(8+\epsilon)/4}}.

(76)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle^{2}\mathbf{X}_{l}\mathbf{X}_{l}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle^{2}\mathbf{X}_{1}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{lj}^{2}\mathbf{S}_{l}\mathbf{S}_{l}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}S_{1j}^{2}\mathbf{S}_{1}\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}\|\leq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}\|\leq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle~+\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}\|\geq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}\|\geq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert.

The rest of the proof is identical to that of Lemma
12
. By matrix Bernstein inequality, we can bound

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}\|\leq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}\|\leq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\dfrac{Cdt}{n}}

(77)

with probability at least
1-\exp(-t)
.

For the second term, as before, let us define

M^{\prime\prime}:=\left\{1\leq l\leq n:\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}\|\geq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right\}.

It can be checked by (
76
) that

\mathbb{E}(M^{\prime\prime})\leq(nd)^{-\epsilon/8}+n^{-2/3-\epsilon/4},\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\mathbb{P}(M^{\prime\prime}\geq C)\leq\exp(-n^{2/3})

by Chernoff bound for binomial random variables. Following the steps of (
A.4
) , we then have

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{l(j)}\|\geq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{n}\cdot|M^{\prime\prime}|\cdot\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}|\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}|^{2}\cdot\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\|\mathbf{S}_{l}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/2}}{n}=C\sqrt{\dfrac{d}{n}}

(78)

with probability at least
1-(nd)^{-\epsilon/8}-n^{-\epsilon/2}
, where we use the bounds from (
72
) and (
A.4
). The third term can be bounded by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality through an argument identical to (
A.4
). In particular,

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)})^{\top}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}\|\geq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left(\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{4}\right)^{1/2}\cdot\left(\mathbb{P}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1(j)}\|\geq\dfrac{C(nd)^{1/4}}{(\log d)}\right)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left(\mathbb{E}S_{1(j)}^{8}\langle\mathbf{S}_{1(j)},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{8}\right)^{1/4}\cdot\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\dfrac{C}{\sqrt{n}}

using (
76
) with
t=\log d
in the last step. Taking a union bound over
j\in[d]
in the first term (note that the other terms have been bounded uniformly in
j
), we finish the proof.
∎

Lemma 15
.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
be
n
independent copies of a random vector
\mathbf{X}
such that
{\cal L}(\mathbf{X})\in{\cal P}_{\rm ICA}(\mathbf{A};\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2})
for
\epsilon,M_{1},M_{2}>0
. Given any
\delta\in(0,1)
, we have for any
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
, that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max\limits_{1\leq j\leq d}~\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle^{3}\mathbf{X}_{l}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle^{3}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right\rVert\geq C\sqrt{\dfrac{d(\log(1/\delta))}{n}}\right)~\leq\dfrac{\delta}{2}+(nd)^{-\epsilon/8}+n^{-\epsilon/2}.

Proof.

As before,
\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle=S_{lj}
. We define the random vectors

\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}:=\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle^{3}\mathbf{S}_{l}=S_{lj}^{3}\mathbf{S}_{l}
.
Then,

\|\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}\|^{2}=S_{lj}^{8}+(d-1)S_{lj}^{6}+\sum_{i\neq j}S_{lj}^{6}(S_{li}^{2}-1).

By Markov’s inequality, for any
t>d^{-1/2}
,

\mathbb{P}(|S_{lj}|\geq(ndt)^{1/8})\leq\left(ndt\right)^{-(1+\epsilon/8)}.

Moreover, since
\{S_{l(j)}:1\leq j\leq d\}
are independent,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\sum_{i\neq j}S_{lj}^{6}(S_{li}^{2}-1)|^{\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{6}}=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}|S_{lj}|^{8+\epsilon}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{i\neq j}(S_{li}^{2}-1)\right\rvert^{\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{6}}\right)\leq C\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{i\neq j}(S_{li}^{2}-1)\right\rvert^{\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{3}}\right)\right\rvert^{1/2}\leq~Cd^{\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{12}}.

using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, along with (
91
) for
\alpha=2(8+\epsilon)/3
. Thus by Markov inequality, we also have, for any
t>d^{-1/2}
, that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{i\neq j}S_{lj}^{6}(S_{li}^{2}-1)\right\rvert\geq Cndt\right)\leq\dfrac{Cd^{(8+\epsilon)/12}}{(ndt)^{(8+\epsilon)/6}}=\left(nt\right)^{-\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{6}}d^{-\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{12}}.

Thus, for any
t>d^{-1/2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}\|^{2}\geq Cndt\right)\leq

\displaystyle~\left(nt\right)^{-\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{6}}d^{1-\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{12}}+\left(nt\right)^{-\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{8}}d^{-\tfrac{\epsilon}{8}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~n^{-\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{6}}d^{-\tfrac{8+7\epsilon}{12}}t^{-\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{6}}+n^{-\tfrac{8+\epsilon}{8}}d^{-\tfrac{\epsilon}{8}}t^{-\tfrac{(8+\epsilon)}{8}}

(79)

We then use the truncation strategy to bound

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{X}_{l},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle^{3}\mathbf{X}_{l}-\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{a}_{j}\rangle^{3}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}\mathbbm{1}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}\|\leq\dfrac{C\sqrt{nd}}{(\log d)}\right)-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{1(j)}\mathbbm{1}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathbf{Y}_{1(j)}\|\leq\dfrac{C\sqrt{nd}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle~+\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert\dfrac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{Y}_{lj}\mathbbm{1}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\|\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}\|\geq\dfrac{C\sqrt{nd}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert+\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{1(j)}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|\mathbf{Y}_{1(j)}\|\geq\dfrac{C\sqrt{nd}}{(\log d)}\right)\right\rVert.

Note that
{\rm trace}({\rm Var}(\mathbf{Y}_{l(j)}))\leq Cd
. Then, we bound the first term using a vector Bernstein inequality to get

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq d}
